Part A (10 Short Answers × 3 Marks = 30M): Allocate 5 mins per answer (50 mins total) — target ½ to 1 page highlighting the key formula and core definition.
Part B (10 Essay Answers × 7 Marks = 70M): Allocate 12 mins per answer (120 mins total) — target 1.5 to 2 pages with schematic circuit/block diagrams and step-by-step derivations.
Strategy & Review Buffer (10 mins): 5 mins at start for question choice planning + 5 mins at end for question numbering and equation verification.

Course Code: EET401

Advanced Control Systems

Complete university previous year questions, continuous LTI and non-linear system state models, solution of state equations, Cayley-Hamilton state transition matrices, Kalman controllability/observability, Ackermann state feedback pole placement, Luenberger observers, describing functions for non-linear saturation, phase plane singular point analysis, and Lyapunov stability theorems.

60fps Interactive Simulators: Advanced Control Systems

Interact with state space dynamics, nonlinear limit cycles, and closed-loop pole placement in real time using the slider bars.

💡 Exam Insight: Click anywhere on the 2D grid to inject state disturbances $(x_1, x_2)$! Watch the trajectory automatically spiral onto the stable limit cycle orbit where non-linear energy dissipation balances energy generation.
Time-Domain Step Response $x_1(t)$ & Velocity $x_2(t)$
Complex $s$-Plane Closed-Loop Poles $s = -\zeta\omega_n \pm j\omega_d$
Underdamped ($\zeta < 1$)
💡 Exam Insight (Ackermann's Formula): As you increase damping ratio $\zeta$ past $1.0$, the complex conjugate poles split into two distinct real poles on the negative real axis (Overdamped), completely eliminating overshoot $M_p = e^{-\pi\zeta/\sqrt{1-\zeta^2}}$.
Module 1

State Space Analysis Basics, Modeling & Canonical Forms

9 Elaborated University Questions & Answers

3 Marks (Short Answer)
Short Answer ID: ACS-Q1

Explain the following state space concepts with appropriate mathematical expressions: (i) State, (ii) State variables, (iii) State vector, and (iv) State space. State Space concepts enthokke aanu? (State, State Variables, State Vector, State Space) എന്തൊക്കെയാണ് State Space അടിസ്ഥാന ആശയങ്ങൾ? (State, State Variables, State Vector, State Space)

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = A\mathbf{x}(t) + B\mathbf{u}(t), \quad \mathbf{y}(t) = C\mathbf{x}(t) + D\mathbf{u}(t)$$

State = Minimum set of variables at $t_0$ needed to predict the future behavior of the system.

⚡ ⚡ Key Equation & Ormavekkan
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = A\mathbf{x}(t) + B\mathbf{u}(t), \quad \mathbf{y}(t) = C\mathbf{x}(t) + D\mathbf{u}(t)$$

Ormavekkan: State enn paranjal dynamic system-te memory aanu (energy store cheyyunna $i_L, v_C$ pole). Present state-um input-um arinjaal future motham predict cheyyam.

⚡ ⚡ Key Equation & ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = A\mathbf{x}(t) + B\mathbf{u}(t), \quad \mathbf{y}(t) = C\mathbf{x}(t) + D\mathbf{u}(t)$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: State എന്നാൽ സിസ്റ്റത്തിന്റെ dynamic memory ആണ് (ഊർജ്ജം സൂക്ഷിക്കുന്ന $i_L, v_C$ പോലെ). നിലവിലെ അവസ്ഥയും ഇൻപുട്ടും അറിഞ്ഞാൽ ഭാവി പൂർണ്ണമായി മുൻകൂട്ടി കണക്കാക്കാം.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. State of a System:

  • The state of a dynamic system is defined as the minimal set of variables $\{x_1(t), x_2(t), \dots, x_n(t)\}$ such that the knowledge of these variables at any initial time $t = t_0$, together with the knowledge of input excitation $u(t)$ for all $t \ge t_0$, completely determines the future state and output of the system for any time $t > t_0$.
  • It represents the internal dynamic memory of the system.

2. State Variables:

  • The individual variables that make up the state are called state variables.
  • They are the minimum number of linearly independent variables that represent all independent energy storage elements in the system.
  • Examples:
    • In electrical systems: Inductor current $i_L(t)$ (magnetic energy) and capacitor voltage $v_C(t)$ (electric energy).
    • In mechanical systems: Displacement $y(t)$ (potential energy) and velocity $\dot{y}(t)$ (kinetic energy).

3. State Vector:

  • If an $n$-th order system requires $n$ state variables, they are grouped together into an $n \times 1$ column vector called the state vector $\mathbf{x}(t)$: $$\mathbf{x}(t) = \begin{bmatrix} x_1(t) \\ x_2(t) \\ \vdots \\ x_n(t) \end{bmatrix} \in \mathbb{R}^n$$

4. State Space:

  • The $n$-dimensional vector space whose coordinate axes are the individual state variables $x_1, x_2, \dots, x_n$ is called the state space.
  • At any time instant $t$, the operating condition of the system is represented by a single point called the state point.
  • As time progresses, this point moves and traces a continuous curve called the state trajectory.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

4 Main Terms: Exam paper-il ee 4 terms-um heading aakki ezhuthanam:

  • 1. State: Oru dynamic system-te minimum number of variables $\{x_1, x_2, \dots, x_n\}$ aanu state. Time $t_0$-il ee variables-inte value-um, $t \ge t_0$-il kodukkunna input $u(t)$-um arinjaal, future-il system-te ella behaviour-um output-um 100% theerumanikkan pattum.
  • 2. State Variables: System-il energy store cheythu vekkunna independent physical elements-inte variables aanu ithu. Electrical circuit-il inductor current $i_L$-um capacitor voltage $v_C$-um; mechanical-il position-um velocity-um.
  • 3. State Vector: Ee ella state variables-ineyum koodi oru single column matrix aakki ezhuthiyathaanu state vector: $\mathbf{x}(t) = [x_1, x_2, \dots, x_n]^T \in \mathbb{R}^n$.
  • 4. State Space: Ee $n$ state variables coordinate axes aayi varunna $n$-dimensional vector space aanu State Space. Oro samayathum system-te state ee space-il oru single point aayirikkum!
📝 Exact English Text for University Examination:

1. State of a System:

  • The state of a dynamic system is defined as the minimal set of variables $\{x_1(t), x_2(t), \dots, x_n(t)\}$ such that the knowledge of these variables at any initial time $t = t_0$, together with the knowledge of input excitation $u(t)$ for all $t \ge t_0$, completely determines the future state and output of the system for any time $t > t_0$.
  • It represents the internal dynamic memory of the system.

2. State Variables:

  • The individual variables that make up the state are called state variables.
  • They are the minimum number of linearly independent variables that represent all independent energy storage elements in the system.
  • Examples:
    • In electrical systems: Inductor current $i_L(t)$ (magnetic energy) and capacitor voltage $v_C(t)$ (electric energy).
    • In mechanical systems: Displacement $y(t)$ (potential energy) and velocity $\dot{y}(t)$ (kinetic energy).

3. State Vector:

  • If an $n$-th order system requires $n$ state variables, they are grouped together into an $n \times 1$ column vector called the state vector $\mathbf{x}(t)$: $$\mathbf{x}(t) = \begin{bmatrix} x_1(t) \\ x_2(t) \\ \vdots \\ x_n(t) \end{bmatrix} \in \mathbb{R}^n$$

4. State Space:

  • The $n$-dimensional vector space whose coordinate axes are the individual state variables $x_1, x_2, \dots, x_n$ is called the state space.
  • At any time instant $t$, the operating condition of the system is represented by a single point called the state point.
  • As time progresses, this point moves and traces a continuous curve called the state trajectory.

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പഠന സഹായി

4 പ്രധാന നിർവ്വചനങ്ങൾ:

  • 1. State (അവസ്ഥ): സിസ്റ്റത്തിന്റെ ഭാവി പ്രതികരണങ്ങൾ കൃത്യമായി മുൻകൂട്ടി കാണാൻ ആവശ്യമായ ഏറ്റവും കുറഞ്ഞ വേരിയബിളുകളുടെ കൂട്ടമാണ് state.
  • 2. State Variables: സിസ്റ്റത്തിൽ ഊർജ്ജം സംഭരിച്ചു വെക്കുന്ന ഘടകങ്ങളുടെ വേരിയബിളുകൾ (ഇൻഡക്ടറിലെ കറന്റ് $i_L$, കപ്പാസിറ്ററിലെ വോൾട്ടേജ് $v_C$).
  • 3. State Vector: എല്ലാ state variables-നെയും ഒരു കോളത്തിൽ അടുക്കി എഴുതിയ മാട്രിക്സ്: $\mathbf{x}(t) = [x_1, x_2, \dots, x_n]^T$.
  • 4. State Space: ഈ വേരിയബിളുകൾ അക്ഷങ്ങളായി വരുന്ന $n$-ഡൈമൻഷണൽ ഗ്രാഫിക്കൽ സ്പേസ്.

  • The state of a dynamic system is defined as the minimal set of variables $\{x_1(t), x_2(t), \dots, x_n(t)\}$ such that the knowledge of these variables at any initial time $t = t_0$, together with the knowledge of input excitation $u(t)$ for all $t \ge t_0$, completely determines the future state and output of the system for any time $t > t_0$.
  • It represents the internal dynamic memory of the system.

2. State Variables:

  • The individual variables that make up the state are called state variables.
  • They are the minimum number of linearly independent variables that represent all independent energy storage elements in the system.
  • Examples:
    • In electrical systems: Inductor current $i_L(t)$ (magnetic energy) and capacitor voltage $v_C(t)$ (electric energy).
    • In mechanical systems: Displacement $y(t)$ (potential energy) and velocity $\dot{y}(t)$ (kinetic energy).

3. State Vector:

  • If an $n$-th order system requires $n$ state variables, they are grouped together into an $n \times 1$ column vector called the state vector $\mathbf{x}(t)$: $$\mathbf{x}(t) = \begin{bmatrix} x_1(t) \\ x_2(t) \\ \vdots \\ x_n(t) \end{bmatrix} \in \mathbb{R}^n$$

  • As time progresses, this point moves and traces a continuous curve called the state trajectory.

Figure: Figure 1.1: Multi-dimensional State Space Representation with Trajectory Vector

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

Think of tracking a cricket ball in flight. If you know its current position $(x, y, z)$ and its velocity $(v_x, v_y, v_z)$ right now, plus gravity and wind, you can predict its entire path. You don't need to know where the bowler was standing 10 minutes ago!

Here, position and velocity are the state variables. Together they form the state vector. The 3D space in which the ball flies is the state space, and the arc traced by the ball is the state trajectory.

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

Think of tracking a cricket ball in flight. Ee nimisham ball-inte current position $(x, y, z)$-um speed $(v_x, v_y, v_z)$-um namukku ariyaamenkil, pinne kaattum gravity-um koodi nokkiyaal ball engottaanu pokunnath ennu 100% parayan pattum. 5 minute munpu ball evidairunnu ennu nokkenda aavashyame illa!

Ee position-um speed-um aanu state variables. Avaye oru list aakki ezhuthiyathaanu state vector. Athu varayunna graph aanu state space!

3 Marks (Short Answer)
Short Answer ID: ACS-Q2

What are phase variables? Formulate the continuous-time state-space representation of an n-th order differential equation using phase variables. Enthane phase variable enn ariyo? Oru n-th order differential equation engane phase variables vechu state space aakkam? എന്താണ് phase variables എന്ന് അറിയോ? ഒരു n-th order differential equation എങ്ങനെ phase variables ഉപയോഗിച്ച് state space രൂപത്തിലാക്കാം?

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$x_1 = y, \quad x_2 = \dot{y}, \quad \dots, \quad x_n = y^{(n-1)}$$

Phase variables are chosen by setting the output as first state and each next state as its derivative.

⚡ ⚡ Key Equation & Ormavekkan
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$x_1 = y, \quad x_2 = \dot{y}, \quad x_3 = \ddot{y}, \quad \dots, \quad x_n = y^{(n-1)}$$

Ormavekkan: Output adya state ayum adutha ella state um adinte derivative ayumane phase variable choose cheyanathe. Matrix $A$-yude last row direct aayi characteristic equation-te coefficients tharum (Companion form)!

⚡ ⚡ Key Equation & ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$x_1 = y, \quad x_2 = \dot{y}, \quad x_3 = \ddot{y}, \quad \dots, \quad x_n = y^{(n-1)}$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: Output ആദ്യ state ആയും അടുത്ത എല്ലാ state-ഉം അതിന്റെ derivative ആയുമാണ് phase variable choose ചെയ്യുന്നത്. Matrix $A$-യുടെ അവസാന വരി നേരിട്ട് differential equation-ന്റെ coefficients നൽകും (Companion form)!

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Definition of Phase Variables:

  • Phase variables are a particular set of state variables where each successive state variable is defined as the exact time derivative of the previous variable, starting with the system output $y(t)$: $$x_1 = y(t)$$ $$x_2 = \dot{y}(t) = \dot{x}_1$$ $$x_3 = \ddot{y}(t) = \dot{x}_2$$ $$\vdots$$ $$x_n = y^{(n-1)}(t) = \dot{x}_{n-1}$$

2. State-Space Formulation from Differential Equation:

Consider a general $n$-th order linear differential equation with constant coefficients: $$y^{(n)} + a_{n-1}y^{(n-1)} + \dots + a_1\dot{y} + a_0 y = b_0 u(t)$$

Express the highest derivative $\dot{x}_n = y^{(n)}$ in terms of other state variables and input: $$\dot{x}_n = -a_0 x_1 - a_1 x_2 - \dots - a_{n-1} x_n + b_0 u(t)$$

3. Matrix Representation (Companion Form):

$$\begin{bmatrix} \dot{x}_1 \\ \dot{x}_2 \\ \vdots \\ \dot{x}_{n-1} \\ \dot{x}_n \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 1 & 0 & \dots & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & 1 & \dots & 0 \\ \vdots & \vdots & \vdots & \ddots & \vdots \\ 0 & 0 & 0 & \dots & 1 \\ -a_0 & -a_1 & -a_2 & \dots & -a_{n-1} \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1 \\ x_2 \\ \vdots \\ x_{n-1} \\ x_n \end{bmatrix} + \begin{bmatrix} 0 \\ 0 \\ \vdots \\ 0 \\ b_0 \end{bmatrix} u(t)$$ $$y(t) = \begin{bmatrix} 1 & 0 & \dots & 0 \end{bmatrix} \mathbf{x}(t)$$

Key Advantages: Matrix $A$ is in Bush form (Companion form). The last row directly gives the coefficients of the characteristic equation, which makes checking controllability very simple.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

Campus Tone Note: Exam-il English-il ezhuthanda points step-by-step aayi ivide explain cheythittundu. Formula-sum matrix-um exact aayi nokkuka!

1. Enthaanu Phase Variables? (Definition)

Phase variables enn paranjaal, nammude system output $y(t)$-ne adyathe state variable $x_1$ aayi set cheyyunu. Athu kazhinju varunna oro state-um തൊട്ടു munpathe state-inte exact time derivative aayi choose cheyyunu:

$$x_1 = y(t), \quad x_2 = \dot{y} = \dot{x}_1, \quad x_3 = \ddot{y} = \dot{x}_2, \quad \dots, \quad x_n = y^{(n-1)} = \dot{x}_{n-1}$$

2. Companion Matrix Formulation:

General equation: $y^{(n)} + a_{n-1}y^{(n-1)} + \dots + a_0 y = b_0 u(t)$.

Highest derivative $\dot{x}_n = -a_0 x_1 - a_1 x_2 - \dots - a_{n-1} x_n + b_0 u$.

Matrix $A$-yude super-diagonal-il 1s varum, bottom row-il negative coefficients $[-a_0, -a_1, \dots, -a_{n-1}]$ varum!

📝 Exact English Text for University Examination:

1. Definition of Phase Variables:

  • Phase variables are a particular set of state variables where each successive state variable is defined as the exact time derivative of the previous variable, starting with the system output $y(t)$: $$x_1 = y(t)$$ $$x_2 = \dot{y}(t) = \dot{x}_1$$ $$x_3 = \ddot{y}(t) = \dot{x}_2$$ $$\vdots$$ $$x_n = y^{(n-1)}(t) = \dot{x}_{n-1}$$

2. State-Space Formulation from Differential Equation:

Consider a general $n$-th order linear differential equation with constant coefficients: $$y^{(n)} + a_{n-1}y^{(n-1)} + \dots + a_1\dot{y} + a_0 y = b_0 u(t)$$

Express the highest derivative $\dot{x}_n = y^{(n)}$ in terms of other state variables and input: $$\dot{x}_n = -a_0 x_1 - a_1 x_2 - \dots - a_{n-1} x_n + b_0 u(t)$$

3. Matrix Representation (Companion Form):

$$\begin{bmatrix} \dot{x}_1 \\ \dot{x}_2 \\ \vdots \\ \dot{x}_{n-1} \\ \dot{x}_n \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 1 & 0 & \dots & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & 1 & \dots & 0 \\ \vdots & \vdots & \vdots & \ddots & \vdots \\ 0 & 0 & 0 & \dots & 1 \\ -a_0 & -a_1 & -a_2 & \dots & -a_{n-1} \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1 \\ x_2 \\ \vdots \\ x_{n-1} \\ x_n \end{bmatrix} + \begin{bmatrix} 0 \\ 0 \\ \vdots \\ 0 \\ b_0 \end{bmatrix} u(t)$$ $$y(t) = \begin{bmatrix} 1 & 0 & \dots & 0 \end{bmatrix} \mathbf{x}(t)$$

Key Advantages: Matrix $A$ is in Bush form (Companion form). The last row directly gives the coefficients of the characteristic equation, which makes checking controllability very simple.

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പഠന സഹായി

പരീക്ഷാ ഗൈഡ്: പ്രധാന സമവാക്യങ്ങളും സ്റ്റെപ്പുകളും താഴെ കാണുക:

സിസ്റ്റത്തിന്റെ output $y(t)$-നെ $x_1$ ആയും, തുടർന്നുള്ള ഓരോ ഡെറിവേറ്റീവുകളെയും അടുത്തടുത്ത states ആയും എടുക്കുന്ന രീതിയാണ് phase variables.

$$\dot{\mathbf{x}} = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 1 & \dots & 0 \\ \vdots & \vdots & \ddots & \vdots \\ -a_0 & -a_1 & \dots & -a_{n-1} \end{bmatrix}\mathbf{x} + \begin{bmatrix} 0 \\ \vdots \\ b_0 \end{bmatrix}u$$
📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

1. Definition of Phase Variables:

  • Phase variables are a particular set of state variables where each successive state variable is defined as the exact time derivative of the previous variable, starting with the system output $y(t)$: $$x_1 = y(t)$$ $$x_2 = \dot{y}(t) = \dot{x}_1$$ $$x_3 = \ddot{y}(t) = \dot{x}_2$$ $$\vdots$$ $$x_n = y^{(n-1)}(t) = \dot{x}_{n-1}$$

2. State-Space Formulation from Differential Equation:

Consider a general $n$-th order linear differential equation with constant coefficients: $$y^{(n)} + a_{n-1}y^{(n-1)} + \dots + a_1\dot{y} + a_0 y = b_0 u(t)$$

Express the highest derivative $\dot{x}_n = y^{(n)}$ in terms of other state variables and input: $$\dot{x}_n = -a_0 x_1 - a_1 x_2 - \dots - a_{n-1} x_n + b_0 u(t)$$

3. Matrix Representation (Companion Form):

$$\begin{bmatrix} \dot{x}_1 \\ \dot{x}_2 \\ \vdots \\ \dot{x}_{n-1} \\ \dot{x}_n \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 1 & 0 & \dots & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & 1 & \dots & 0 \\ \vdots & \vdots & \vdots & \ddots & \vdots \\ 0 & 0 & 0 & \dots & 1 \\ -a_0 & -a_1 & -a_2 & \dots & -a_{n-1} \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1 \\ x_2 \\ \vdots \\ x_{n-1} \\ x_n \end{bmatrix} + \begin{bmatrix} 0 \\ 0 \\ \vdots \\ 0 \\ b_0 \end{bmatrix} u(t)$$ $$y(t) = \begin{bmatrix} 1 & 0 & \dots & 0 \end{bmatrix} \mathbf{x}(t)$$

Key Advantages: Matrix $A$ is in Bush form (Companion form). The last row directly gives the coefficients of the characteristic equation, which makes checking controllability very simple.

Figure: Figure 1.2: Successive Integrator Chain Generating Phase Variables

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

If you have an equation with position, speed, and acceleration (like a moving car), phase variables simply say: let $x_1$ be position, let $x_2$ be speed ($\dot{x}_1$), and let $x_3$ be acceleration ($\dot{x}_2$). It converts one complicated high-order equation into a ladder of simple first-order integrator steps!

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

Oru car drive cheyyunna karyam alochichu nokku. Car-inte road-ile position aanu $x_1$. Car-inte speed (position-te first derivative $\dot{x}_1$) aanu $x_2$. Car-inte acceleration (speed-inte derivative $\dot{x}_2$) aanu $x_3$.

Oru valiya complicated differential equation-ine cheriya simple integrator steps aakki maatan ulla super techniqua-anu phase variables! Output adya state ayum adutha ella state um adinte derivative ayumane phase variable choose cheyanathe. Simple alle?

🇮🇳 മലയാളം ▼

ഒരു കാർ ഓടിക്കുന്ന കാര്യം ആലോചിച്ചു നോക്കൂ. റോഡിലെ കാറിന്റെ സ്ഥാനമാണ് $x_1$. കാറിന്റെ വേഗത $x_2$. കാറിന്റെ ആക്സിലറേഷൻ $x_3$. വളരെ സങ്കീർണ്ണമായ ഒരു വലിയ സമവാക്യത്തെ ലളിതമായ പടവുകളാക്കി മാറ്റുന്ന വിദ്യയാണിത്!

3 Marks (Short Answer)
Short Answer ID: ACS-Q3

Draw and explain the general block diagram representation of a continuous-time LTI system described by state and output equations. LTI State-Space system-te continuous-time block diagram engane varakkam? LTI State-Space സിസ്റ്റത്തിന്റെ ബ്ലോക്ക് ഡയഗ്രം എങ്ങനെ വരയ്ക്കാം?

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$\dot{\mathbf{x}} = A\mathbf{x} + B\mathbf{u}, \quad \mathbf{y} = C\mathbf{x} + D\mathbf{u}$$

Core element: Bank of integrators $\frac{1}{s}$ converts $\dot{\mathbf{x}}$ into $\mathbf{x}$.

📌 ⚡ Integrator Heart & 4 Matrices
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\dot{\mathbf{x}} = A\mathbf{x} + B\mathbf{u}, \quad \mathbf{y} = C\mathbf{x} + D\mathbf{u}$$

Ormavekkan: State variables eppozhum integrators ($\frac{1}{s}$)-te output aayirikkum. $A$ feedback loop aanu, $B$ input gain, $C$ output observation, $D$ direct feedthrough.

📌 ⚡ പ്രധാന മാട്രിക്സ് ബന്ധങ്ങൾ
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\dot{\mathbf{x}} = A\mathbf{x} + B\mathbf{u}, \quad \mathbf{y} = C\mathbf{x} + D\mathbf{u}$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: State variables എപ്പോഴും ഇന്റഗ്രേറ്ററുകളുടെ ഔട്ട്പുട്ട് ആയിരിക്കും. $A, B, C, D$ മാട്രിക്സുകളാണ് മറ്റ് കണക്ഷനുകൾ.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Governing Equations:

Any continuous-time Linear Time-Invariant (LTI) system is represented in state-space by: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = A\mathbf{x}(t) + B\mathbf{u}(t) \quad \text{--- [State Equation]}$$ $$\mathbf{y}(t) = C\mathbf{x}(t) + D\mathbf{u}(t) \quad \text{--- [Output Equation]}$$

2. Description of Block Diagram Elements:

  • Integrator Block ($\int$ or $\frac{1}{s}$): The heart of the diagram. The input to the integrator is the state derivative vector $\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t)$, and its output is the state vector $\mathbf{x}(t)$. For an $n$-th order system, it represents $n$ parallel integrators.
  • State Matrix $A$ ($n \times n$): Feedback gain block feeding $\mathbf{x}(t)$ back to the summer to calculate internal dynamics $A\mathbf{x}(t)$.
  • Input Matrix $B$ ($n \times m$): Multiplies input vector $\mathbf{u}(t)$ to couple external inputs into state derivatives $B\mathbf{u}(t)$.
  • Output Matrix $C$ ($p \times n$): Multiplies state vector $\mathbf{x}(t)$ to determine how internal states contribute to measured outputs $C\mathbf{x}(t)$.
  • Direct Transmission Matrix $D$ ($p \times m$): Direct feedthrough gain connecting input $\mathbf{u}(t)$ directly to output $\mathbf{y}(t)$ without passing through integrators (often zero in physical systems).
  • Summing Points: One summer adds $A\mathbf{x} + B\mathbf{u} = \dot{\mathbf{x}}$, and another summer adds $C\mathbf{x} + D\mathbf{u} = \mathbf{y}$.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

Block Diagram Connection Logic:

  1. Nammal center-il $n$ integrators ($\int$ or $\frac{1}{s}$) varakkunnu. Integrator-te input $\dot{\mathbf{x}}$ aanu, output $\mathbf{x}$ aanu!
  2. Input $\mathbf{u}$ matrix $B$ vazhi oru summing junction-ilottu pokunnu.
  3. State $\mathbf{x}$ thirichu matrix $A$ vazhi loop aayi summing junction-il add cheythu $\dot{\mathbf{x}} = A\mathbf{x} + B\mathbf{u}$ create cheyyunnu.
  4. Output junction-il $C\mathbf{x}$-um $D\mathbf{u}$-um add cheythu $\mathbf{y}$ undakkunnu.

1. Governing Equations:

Any continuous-time Linear Time-Invariant (LTI) system is represented in state-space by: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = A\mathbf{x}(t) + B\mathbf{u}(t) \quad \text{--- [State Equation]}$$ $$\mathbf{y}(t) = C\mathbf{x}(t) + D\mathbf{u}(t) \quad \text{--- [Output Equation]}$$

2. Description of Block Diagram Elements:

  • Integrator Block ($\int$ or $\frac{1}{s}$): The heart of the diagram. The input to the integrator is the state derivative vector $\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t)$, and its output is the state vector $\mathbf{x}(t)$. For an $n$-th order system, it represents $n$ parallel integrators.
  • State Matrix $A$ ($n \times n$): Feedback gain block feeding $\mathbf{x}(t)$ back to the summer to calculate internal dynamics $A\mathbf{x}(t)$.
  • Input Matrix $B$ ($n \times m$): Multiplies input vector $\mathbf{u}(t)$ to couple external inputs into state derivatives $B\mathbf{u}(t)$.
  • Output Matrix $C$ ($p \times n$): Multiplies state vector $\mathbf{x}(t)$ to determine how internal states contribute to measured outputs $C\mathbf{x}(t)$.
  • Direct Transmission Matrix $D$ ($p \times m$): Direct feedthrough gain connecting input $\mathbf{u}(t)$ directly to output $\mathbf{y}(t)$ without passing through integrators (often zero in physical systems).
  • Summing Points: One summer adds $A\mathbf{x} + B\mathbf{u} = \dot{\mathbf{x}}$, and another summer adds $C\mathbf{x} + D\mathbf{u} = \mathbf{y}$.

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പ്രാദേശിക പഠന സഹായി

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പഠന സഹായി

ഇന്റഗ്രേറ്ററുകൾക്ക് മുൻപിൽ $\dot{\mathbf{x}}$-ഉം, ഔട്ട്പുട്ടിൽ $\mathbf{x}$-ഉം വരുന്നു. മാട്രിക്സ് $A$ ഫീഡ്‌ബാക്ക് ലൂപ്പായും, $B$ ഇൻപുട്ട് ഗെയിനായും, $C$ ഔട്ട്പുട്ട് മാട്രിക്സായും പ്രവർത്തിക്കുന്നു.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

1. Governing Equations:

Any continuous-time Linear Time-Invariant (LTI) system is represented in state-space by: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = A\mathbf{x}(t) + B\mathbf{u}(t) \quad \text{--- [State Equation]}$$ $$\mathbf{y}(t) = C\mathbf{x}(t) + D\mathbf{u}(t) \quad \text{--- [Output Equation]}$$

2. Description of Block Diagram Elements:

  • Integrator Block ($\int$ or $\frac{1}{s}$): The heart of the diagram. The input to the integrator is the state derivative vector $\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t)$, and its output is the state vector $\mathbf{x}(t)$. For an $n$-th order system, it represents $n$ parallel integrators.
  • State Matrix $A$ ($n \times n$): Feedback gain block feeding $\mathbf{x}(t)$ back to the summer to calculate internal dynamics $A\mathbf{x}(t)$.
  • Input Matrix $B$ ($n \times m$): Multiplies input vector $\mathbf{u}(t)$ to couple external inputs into state derivatives $B\mathbf{u}(t)$.
  • Output Matrix $C$ ($p \times n$): Multiplies state vector $\mathbf{x}(t)$ to determine how internal states contribute to measured outputs $C\mathbf{x}(t)$.
  • Direct Transmission Matrix $D$ ($p \times m$): Direct feedthrough gain connecting input $\mathbf{u}(t)$ directly to output $\mathbf{y}(t)$ without passing through integrators (often zero in physical systems).
  • Summing Points: One summer adds $A\mathbf{x} + B\mathbf{u} = \dot{\mathbf{x}}$, and another summer adds $C\mathbf{x} + D\mathbf{u} = \mathbf{y}$.

Figure: Figure 1.3: Standard Integrator-Based Block Diagram of Continuous LTI System

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

Think of the integrator as a water bucket. The water flowing in is the rate of change ($\dot{\mathbf{x}}$), and the water level inside the bucket is the state ($\mathbf{x}$). The matrix $A$ tells how much water leaks back into the inlet, $B$ is the external tap adding water, and $C$ is the meter measuring the level.

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

Oru water tank alochichu nokku: Tank-ilottu ozhukivarunna vellathinte flow rate aanu $\dot{x}$ (derivative). Tank-inte ullil sambharicha total vellam aanu $x$ (integrated state). Tank oru integrator pole aanu — flow-ne kooti vellathinte level aakkunnu!

ഒരു വാട്ടർ ടാങ്ക് പോലെയാണ് ഇന്റഗ്രേറ്റർ. ഒഴുകിയെത്തുന്ന വെള്ളത്തിന്റെ സ്പീഡ് ($\dot{x}$) ടാങ്കിൽ ചേർന്ന് ജലനിരപ്പായി ($x$) മാറുന്നു!

3 Marks (Short Answer)
Short Answer ID: ACS-Q4

Show that the state space representation of a dynamic system is not unique using similarity transformations. Prove that eigenvalues remain invariant. State space representation unique alla ennu engane prove cheyyam? (Similarity Transformation) സ്റ്റേറ്റ് സ്പേസ് മോഡൽ unique അല്ല എന്ന് എങ്ങനെ തെളിയിക്കാം? (Similarity Transformation)

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$\bar{A} = P^{-1}AP, \quad \bar{B} = P^{-1}B, \quad \bar{C} = CP, \quad \bar{D} = D$$

State-space models are not unique; an infinite number of representations exist by changing state coordinates via non-singular matrix $P$.

📌 ⚡ Similarity Transformation Rules
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\bar{A} = P^{-1}AP, \quad \bar{B} = P^{-1}B, \quad \bar{C} = CP, \quad \bar{D} = D$$

Ormavekkan: Non-singular matrix $P$ vechu state coordinate maattinaalum eigenvalues-um transfer function $G(s)$-um maarilla (Strictly Invariant)!

⚡ ⚡ ട്രാൻസ്ഫോർമേഷൻ സമവാക്യം
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\bar{A} = P^{-1}AP, \quad \bar{B} = P^{-1}B, \quad \bar{C} = CP, \quad \bar{D} = D$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: മാട്രിക്സ് $P$ മാറിയാലും സിസ്റ്റത്തിന്റെ ഐഗൻവാല്യൂസും ട്രാൻസ്ഫർ ഫങ്ഷനും ഒരിക്കലും മാറില്ല.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Principle of Non-Uniqueness:

  • While the input-output transfer function of a physical system is unique, its state-space representation is not unique.
  • There are an infinite number of valid state-space models for the same system because state variables can be chosen in infinitely many coordinate systems.

2. Mathematical Proof via Similarity Transformation:

Let the original state model be: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}} = A\mathbf{x} + B\mathbf{u}, \quad \mathbf{y} = C\mathbf{x} + D\mathbf{u}$$ Define a new state vector $\mathbf{z}(t)$ through a non-singular transformation matrix $P$ ($n \times n$ with $\det(P) \ne 0$): $$\mathbf{x}(t) = P\mathbf{z}(t) \implies \mathbf{z}(t) = P^{-1}\mathbf{x}(t)$$ Differentiating both sides with respect to time: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = P\dot{\mathbf{z}}(t)$$ Substitute $\mathbf{x} = P\mathbf{z}$ and $\dot{\mathbf{x}} = P\dot{\mathbf{z}}$ into the original state equation: $$P\dot{\mathbf{z}}(t) = A(P\mathbf{z}(t)) + B\mathbf{u}(t)$$ Premultiplying both sides by $P^{-1}$: $$\dot{\mathbf{z}}(t) = \left(P^{-1}AP\right)\mathbf{z}(t) + \left(P^{-1}B\right)\mathbf{u}(t)$$ Now substitute $\mathbf{x} = P\mathbf{z}$ into the output equation: $$\mathbf{y}(t) = \left(CP\right)\mathbf{z}(t) + D\mathbf{u}(t)$$ Thus, the new transformed system matrices are: $$\bar{A} = P^{-1}AP, \quad \bar{B} = P^{-1}B, \quad \bar{C} = CP, \quad \bar{D} = D$$ Invariance Property: The eigenvalues and the transfer function $G(s) = \bar{C}(sI - \bar{A})^{-1}\bar{B} + \bar{D} = C(sI - A)^{-1}B + D$ remain strictly identical under any valid transformation $P$.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

Proof Steps: Let $\mathbf{x} = P\mathbf{z}$, where $P$ is an $n \times n$ invertible matrix ($\det(P) \ne 0$).

$\dot{\mathbf{x}} = P\dot{\mathbf{z}} = A P\mathbf{z} + B\mathbf{u}$. Both sides $P^{-1}$ premultiply cheythal: $\dot{\mathbf{z}} = (P^{-1}AP)\mathbf{z} + (P^{-1}B)\mathbf{u} = \bar{A}\mathbf{z} + \bar{B}\mathbf{u}$.

Characteristic equation: $\det(sI - \bar{A}) = \det(sI - P^{-1}AP) = \det(P^{-1}(sI - A)P) = \det(sI - A)$. So poles are identical!

📝 Exact English Text for University Examination:

1. Principle of Non-Uniqueness:

  • While the input-output transfer function of a physical system is unique, its state-space representation is not unique.
  • There are an infinite number of valid state-space models for the same system because state variables can be chosen in infinitely many coordinate systems.

2. Mathematical Proof via Similarity Transformation:

Let the original state model be: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}} = A\mathbf{x} + B\mathbf{u}, \quad \mathbf{y} = C\mathbf{x} + D\mathbf{u}$$ Define a new state vector $\mathbf{z}(t)$ through a non-singular transformation matrix $P$ ($n \times n$ with $\det(P) \ne 0$): $$\mathbf{x}(t) = P\mathbf{z}(t) \implies \mathbf{z}(t) = P^{-1}\mathbf{x}(t)$$ Differentiating both sides with respect to time: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = P\dot{\mathbf{z}}(t)$$ Substitute $\mathbf{x} = P\mathbf{z}$ and $\dot{\mathbf{x}} = P\dot{\mathbf{z}}$ into the original state equation: $$P\dot{\mathbf{z}}(t) = A(P\mathbf{z}(t)) + B\mathbf{u}(t)$$ Premultiplying both sides by $P^{-1}$: $$\dot{\mathbf{z}}(t) = \left(P^{-1}AP\right)\mathbf{z}(t) + \left(P^{-1}B\right)\mathbf{u}(t)$$ Now substitute $\mathbf{x} = P\mathbf{z}$ into the output equation: $$\mathbf{y}(t) = \left(CP\right)\mathbf{z}(t) + D\mathbf{u}(t)$$ Thus, the new transformed system matrices are: $$\bar{A} = P^{-1}AP, \quad \bar{B} = P^{-1}B, \quad \bar{C} = CP, \quad \bar{D} = D$$ Invariance Property: The eigenvalues and the transfer function $G(s) = \bar{C}(sI - \bar{A})^{-1}\bar{B} + \bar{D} = C(sI - A)^{-1}B + D$ remain strictly identical under any valid transformation $P$.

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പ്രാദേശിക പഠന സഹായി

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പഠന സഹായി

$\mathbf{x} = P\mathbf{z}$ എന്ന് നൽകി മാട്രിക്സ് പുനഃക്രമീകരിക്കുമ്പോൾ ഐഗൻവാല്യൂകൾ മാറ്റമില്ലാതെ തുടരുന്നു എന്ന് കാണിക്കുക.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

1. Principle of Non-Uniqueness:

  • While the input-output transfer function of a physical system is unique, its state-space representation is not unique.
  • There are an infinite number of valid state-space models for the same system because state variables can be chosen in infinitely many coordinate systems.

2. Mathematical Proof via Similarity Transformation:

Let the original state model be: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}} = A\mathbf{x} + B\mathbf{u}, \quad \mathbf{y} = C\mathbf{x} + D\mathbf{u}$$ Define a new state vector $\mathbf{z}(t)$ through a non-singular transformation matrix $P$ ($n \times n$ with $\det(P) \ne 0$): $$\mathbf{x}(t) = P\mathbf{z}(t) \implies \mathbf{z}(t) = P^{-1}\mathbf{x}(t)$$ Differentiating both sides with respect to time: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = P\dot{\mathbf{z}}(t)$$ Substitute $\mathbf{x} = P\mathbf{z}$ and $\dot{\mathbf{x}} = P\dot{\mathbf{z}}$ into the original state equation: $$P\dot{\mathbf{z}}(t) = A(P\mathbf{z}(t)) + B\mathbf{u}(t)$$ Premultiplying both sides by $P^{-1}$: $$\dot{\mathbf{z}}(t) = \left(P^{-1}AP\right)\mathbf{z}(t) + \left(P^{-1}B\right)\mathbf{u}(t)$$ Now substitute $\mathbf{x} = P\mathbf{z}$ into the output equation: $$\mathbf{y}(t) = \left(CP\right)\mathbf{z}(t) + D\mathbf{u}(t)$$ Thus, the new transformed system matrices are: $$\bar{A} = P^{-1}AP, \quad \bar{B} = P^{-1}B, \quad \bar{C} = CP, \quad \bar{D} = D$$ Invariance Property: The eigenvalues and the transfer function $G(s) = \bar{C}(sI - \bar{A})^{-1}\bar{B} + \bar{D} = C(sI - A)^{-1}B + D$ remain strictly identical under any valid transformation $P$.

Figure: Figure 1.4: Linear Coordinate Transformation between State Bases

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

Say you want to specify a location in Kochi. You can use latitude/longitude, or you can use distance from Marine Drive in kilometers. The physical spot is the same, but the coordinate numbers look totally different. That is all similarity transformation does: it rotates the coordinate axes without changing the actual physical system.

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

Temperature alakkunna karyam alochikku: Celsius-il paranjaalum Fahrenheit-il paranjaalum choodu maarunnundo? Illa! Units maarum, pakshe physical temperature same aanu. Athu pole aanu coordinate transformation — numbers maarum, system dynamics same aayirikkum!

🇮🇳 മലയാളം ▼

താപനില സെൽഷ്യസിലോ ഫാരൻഹീറ്റിലോ പറഞ്ഞാലും ചൂട് ഒന്നാണ്. അതുപോലെ കോർഡിനേറ്റ് മാറ്റിയാലും സിസ്റ്റം ഫിസിക്സ് മാറുന്നില്ല!

3 Marks (Short Answer)
Short Answer ID: ACS-Q4B

Derive the transfer function matrix G(s) of a continuous-time LTI system from its state-space model. State Space model-il ninnu Transfer Function Matrix engane derive cheyyam? State Space മോഡലിൽ നിന്നും Transfer Function Matrix എങ്ങനെ കണ്ടുപിടിക്കാം?

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$G(s) = C(sI - A)^{-1}B + D = \frac{C \operatorname{adj}(sI - A) B}{\det(sI - A)} + D$$

Poles of $G(s)$ are the eigenvalues of matrix $A$ (roots of $\det(sI - A) = 0$).

📌 ⚡ Key Derivation Result
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$G(s) = C(sI - A)^{-1}B + D = \frac{C \operatorname{adj}(sI - A) B}{\det(sI - A)} + D$$

Main Point: Zero initial condition ($\mathbf{x}(0) = \mathbf{0}$) koduthu Laplace transform edukuka. Denominator $\det(sI - A) = 0$ aayirikkum system poles.

⚡ ⚡ പ്രധാന സമവാക്യം
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$G(s) = C(sI - A)^{-1}B + D = \frac{C \operatorname{adj}(sI - A) B}{\det(sI - A)} + D$$

പ്രധാന പോയിന്റ്: പൂജ്യം പ്രാരംഭ അവസ്ഥ ($\mathbf{x}(0) = \mathbf{0}$) നൽകി Laplace transform എടുക്കുക. ഛേദമായ $\det(sI - A) = 0$ ആണ് സിസ്റ്റത്തിന്റെ ധ്രുവങ്ങൾ.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Given State-Space Equations:
Consider a continuous-time linear time-invariant (LTI) system: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = A\mathbf{x}(t) + B\mathbf{u}(t) \quad \text{--- (1) [State Equation]}$$ $$\mathbf{y}(t) = C\mathbf{x}(t) + D\mathbf{u}(t) \quad \text{--- (2) [Output Equation]}$$ where $\mathbf{x}(t) \in \mathbb{R}^n$, $\mathbf{u}(t) \in \mathbb{R}^m$, $\mathbf{y}(t) \in \mathbb{R}^p$.

2. Assumption:
By definition, transfer function is defined under zero initial conditions ($\mathbf{x}(0) = \mathbf{0}$).

3. Step-by-Step Derivation:

Step 1: Take Laplace Transform of the State Equation
$$\mathcal{L}\{\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t)\} = s\mathbf{X}(s) - \mathbf{x}(0) = A\mathbf{X}(s) + B\mathbf{U}(s)$$ Substituting $\mathbf{x}(0) = \mathbf{0}$: $$s\mathbf{X}(s) - A\mathbf{X}(s) = B\mathbf{U}(s)$$ $$(sI - A)\mathbf{X}(s) = B\mathbf{U}(s)$$ where $I$ is the $n \times n$ identity matrix.

Step 2: Solve for State Vector $\mathbf{X}(s)$
Premultiplying both sides by the inverse matrix $(sI - A)^{-1}$ (the resolvent matrix): $$\mathbf{X}(s) = (sI - A)^{-1} B \mathbf{U}(s) \quad \text{--- (3)}$$

Step 3: Take Laplace Transform of Output Equation
$$\mathbf{Y}(s) = C\mathbf{X}(s) + D\mathbf{U}(s) \quad \text{--- (4)}$$

Step 4: Substitute Equation (3) into Equation (4)
$$\mathbf{Y}(s) = C\left[(sI - A)^{-1} B \mathbf{U}(s)\right] + D\mathbf{U}(s)$$ Factoring out the input transform $\mathbf{U}(s)$: $$\mathbf{Y}(s) = \left[ C(sI - A)^{-1} B + D \right] \mathbf{U}(s)$$

4. Final Transfer Function Matrix:
$$G(s) = \frac{\mathbf{Y}(s)}{\mathbf{U}(s)} = C(sI - A)^{-1} B + D$$ Using the matrix inverse formula $(sI - A)^{-1} = \frac{\operatorname{adj}(sI - A)}{\det(sI - A)}$: $$G(s) = \frac{C \operatorname{adj}(sI - A) B}{\det(sI - A)} + D$$ Significance: The denominator of $G(s)$ is the characteristic polynomial $\det(sI - A) = 0$. Therefore, the poles of the transfer function are the eigenvalues of state matrix $A$.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Derivation Guide

Step 1: $\dot{\mathbf{x}} = A\mathbf{x} + B\mathbf{u} \implies s\mathbf{X}(s) - A\mathbf{X}(s) = B\mathbf{U}(s) \implies (sI - A)\mathbf{X}(s) = B\mathbf{U}(s)$.

Step 2: $\mathbf{X}(s) = (sI - A)^{-1}B\mathbf{U}(s)$.

Step 3: $\mathbf{Y}(s) = C\mathbf{X}(s) + D\mathbf{U}(s) = [C(sI - A)^{-1}B + D]\mathbf{U}(s) \implies G(s) = C(sI - A)^{-1}B + D$.

📝 Exact English Text for University Examination:

1. Given State-Space Equations:
Consider a continuous-time linear time-invariant (LTI) system: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = A\mathbf{x}(t) + B\mathbf{u}(t) \quad \text{--- (1) [State Equation]}$$ $$\mathbf{y}(t) = C\mathbf{x}(t) + D\mathbf{u}(t) \quad \text{--- (2) [Output Equation]}$$ where $\mathbf{x}(t) \in \mathbb{R}^n$, $\mathbf{u}(t) \in \mathbb{R}^m$, $\mathbf{y}(t) \in \mathbb{R}^p$.

2. Assumption:
By definition, transfer function is defined under zero initial conditions ($\mathbf{x}(0) = \mathbf{0}$).

3. Step-by-Step Derivation:

Step 1: Take Laplace Transform of the State Equation
$$\mathcal{L}\{\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t)\} = s\mathbf{X}(s) - \mathbf{x}(0) = A\mathbf{X}(s) + B\mathbf{U}(s)$$ Substituting $\mathbf{x}(0) = \mathbf{0}$: $$s\mathbf{X}(s) - A\mathbf{X}(s) = B\mathbf{U}(s)$$ $$(sI - A)\mathbf{X}(s) = B\mathbf{U}(s)$$ where $I$ is the $n \times n$ identity matrix.

Step 2: Solve for State Vector $\mathbf{X}(s)$
Premultiplying both sides by the inverse matrix $(sI - A)^{-1}$ (the resolvent matrix): $$\mathbf{X}(s) = (sI - A)^{-1} B \mathbf{U}(s) \quad \text{--- (3)}$$

Step 3: Take Laplace Transform of Output Equation
$$\mathbf{Y}(s) = C\mathbf{X}(s) + D\mathbf{U}(s) \quad \text{--- (4)}$$

Step 4: Substitute Equation (3) into Equation (4)
$$\mathbf{Y}(s) = C\left[(sI - A)^{-1} B \mathbf{U}(s)\right] + D\mathbf{U}(s)$$ Factoring out the input transform $\mathbf{U}(s)$: $$\mathbf{Y}(s) = \left[ C(sI - A)^{-1} B + D \right] \mathbf{U}(s)$$

4. Final Transfer Function Matrix:
$$G(s) = \frac{\mathbf{Y}(s)}{\mathbf{U}(s)} = C(sI - A)^{-1} B + D$$ Using the matrix inverse formula $(sI - A)^{-1} = \frac{\operatorname{adj}(sI - A)}{\det(sI - A)}$: $$G(s) = \frac{C \operatorname{adj}(sI - A) B}{\det(sI - A)} + D$$ Significance: The denominator of $G(s)$ is the characteristic polynomial $\det(sI - A) = 0$. Therefore, the poles of the transfer function are the eigenvalues of state matrix $A$.

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പ്രാദേശിക പഠന സഹായി

🇮🇳 മലയാളം ഡെറിവേഷൻ

ലാപ്ലേസ് ട്രാൻസ്ഫോർം എടുത്ത് $\mathbf{X}(s)$ വേർതിരിച്ച് ഔട്ട്പുട്ട് സമവാക്യത്തിൽ ചേർക്കുക.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

1. Given State-Space Equations:

2. Assumption:
By definition, transfer function is defined under zero initial conditions ($\mathbf{x}(0) = \mathbf{0}$).

3. Step-by-Step Derivation:

Step 1: Take Laplace Transform of the State Equation
$$\mathcal{L}\{\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t)\} = s\mathbf{X}(s) - \mathbf{x}(0) = A\mathbf{X}(s) + B\mathbf{U}(s)$$ Substituting $\mathbf{x}(0) = \mathbf{0}$: $$s\mathbf{X}(s) - A\mathbf{X}(s) = B\mathbf{U}(s)$$ $$(sI - A)\mathbf{X}(s) = B\mathbf{U}(s)$$ where $I$ is the $n \times n$ identity matrix.

Step 2: Solve for State Vector $\mathbf{X}(s)$

Step 3: Take Laplace Transform of Output Equation
$$\mathbf{Y}(s) = C\mathbf{X}(s) + D\mathbf{U}(s) \quad \text{--- (4)}$$

Step 4: Substitute Equation (3) into Equation (4)
$$\mathbf{Y}(s) = C\left[(sI - A)^{-1} B \mathbf{U}(s)\right] + D\mathbf{U}(s)$$ Factoring out the input transform $\mathbf{U}(s)$: $$\mathbf{Y}(s) = \left[ C(sI - A)^{-1} B + D \right] \mathbf{U}(s)$$

4. Final Transfer Function Matrix:
$$G(s) = \frac{\mathbf{Y}(s)}{\mathbf{U}(s)} = C(sI - A)^{-1} B + D$$ Using the matrix inverse formula $(sI - A)^{-1} = \frac{\operatorname{adj}(sI - A)}{\det(sI - A)}$: $$G(s) = \frac{C \operatorname{adj}(sI - A) B}{\det(sI - A)} + D$$ Significance: The denominator of $G(s)$ is the characteristic polynomial $\det(sI - A) = 0$. Therefore, the poles of the transfer function are the eigenvalues of state matrix $A$.

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

Classical transfer function treats the system like a sealed black box (input goes in, output comes out). State space looks inside the box at the energy variables $\mathbf{X}(s)$. This derivation is simply using algebra to eliminate $\mathbf{X}(s)$ from the middle and connect input $\mathbf{U}(s)$ directly to output $\mathbf{Y}(s)$.

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

Classical control-il transfer function $G(s)$ oru black box pole aanu — input kodukkunnu, output edukkunnu. State Space ullile energy variables $\mathbf{X}(s)$ thurannu kaanikkunnu. Ee derivation classical-neyum modern state space-neyum connect cheyyunna palam aanu!

7/14 Marks (Long Essay / Derivation)
Long Derivation ID: ACS-Q5

Derive the state-space model of a series RLC circuit excited by an input voltage source, selecting the inductor current and capacitor voltage as state variables. Series RLC circuit-inte State-Space model engane derive cheyyam? ഒരു സീരീസ് RLC സർക്യൂട്ടിന്റെ സ്റ്റേറ്റ് സ്പേസ് മോഡൽ എങ്ങനെ ഉണ്ടാക്കാം?

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$\begin{bmatrix} \dot{i}_L \\ \dot{v}_C \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} -R/L & -1/L \\ 1/C & 0 \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} i_L \\ v_C \end{bmatrix} + \begin{bmatrix} 1/L \\ 0 \end{bmatrix} v(t)$$

Choose inductor current $i_L$ and capacitor voltage $v_C$ as states because they govern stored energy.

⚡ ⚡ RLC State Equations
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\begin{bmatrix} \dot{i}_L \\ \dot{v}_C \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} -R/L & -1/L \\ 1/C & 0 \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} i_L \\ v_C \end{bmatrix} + \begin{bmatrix} 1/L \\ 0 \end{bmatrix} v_{in}(t)$$

Ormavekkan: State variables eppozhum energy storage elements aanu: Inductor current $i_L$ (magnetic energy) and capacitor voltage $v_C$ (electrostatic energy).

📌 ⚡ RLC സ്റ്റേറ്റ് മാട്രിക്സ്
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\dot{\mathbf{x}} = \begin{bmatrix} -R/L & -1/L \\ 1/C & 0 \end{bmatrix}\mathbf{x} + \begin{bmatrix} 1/L \\ 0 \end{bmatrix}v_{in}$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: ഇൻഡക്ടർ കറന്റും ($i_L$) കപ്പാസിറ്റർ വോൾട്ടേജും ($v_C$) ആണ് സ്വാഭാവിക സ്റ്റേറ്റ് വേരിയബിളുകൾ.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Identification of State Variables & Inputs:

  • Consider a series RLC circuit excited by an applied voltage source $v(t)$.
  • The independent energy-storing elements are:
    • Inductor $L$: Stores magnetic energy $E_L = \frac{1}{2} L i_L^2 \implies$ State variable $x_1(t) = i_L(t)$.
    • Capacitor $C$: Stores electric energy $E_C = \frac{1}{2} C v_C^2 \implies$ State variable $x_2(t) = v_C(t)$.
  • Input: $u(t) = v(t)$ (source voltage).
  • Output: Let output $y(t) = v_C(t)$ (voltage across capacitor).

2. Derivation of State Equations:

Equation 1 (Using KVL around the series loop):
$$v(t) = v_R(t) + v_L(t) + v_C(t)$$ Using component relationships $v_R = R i_L$ and $v_L = L \frac{d i_L}{dt}$: $$L \frac{d i_L(t)}{dt} + R i_L(t) + v_C(t) = v(t)$$ Rearranging for $\frac{d i_L}{dt} = \dot{x}_1$: $$\frac{d i_L}{dt} = -\frac{R}{L} i_L - \frac{1}{L} v_C + \frac{1}{L} v(t)$$ $$\dot{x}_1(t) = -\frac{R}{L} x_1(t) - \frac{1}{L} x_2(t) + \frac{1}{L} u(t) \quad \text{--- (1)}$$

Equation 2 (Using Capacitor Current Relation):
Because elements are in series, current through capacitor is $i_L$: $$i_C(t) = C \frac{d v_C(t)}{dt} = i_L(t)$$ Rearranging for $\frac{d v_C}{dt} = \dot{x}_2$: $$\dot{x}_2(t) = \frac{1}{C} x_1(t) \quad \text{--- (2)}$$

3. Final Vector-Matrix State-Space Model:
Combining Equations (1) and (2) into $\dot{\mathbf{x}} = A\mathbf{x} + B\mathbf{u}$: $$\begin{bmatrix} \dot{x}_1(t) \\ \dot{x}_2(t) \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} -\frac{R}{L} & -\frac{1}{L} \\ \frac{1}{C} & 0 \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1(t) \\ x_2(t) \end{bmatrix} + \begin{bmatrix} \frac{1}{L} \\ 0 \end{bmatrix} u(t)$$ Output equation for $y(t) = v_C(t) = x_2(t)$: $$y(t) = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1(t) \\ x_2(t) \end{bmatrix} + [0] u(t)$$ Here: $$A = \begin{bmatrix} -R/L & -1/L \\ 1/C & 0 \end{bmatrix}, \quad B = \begin{bmatrix} 1/L \\ 0 \end{bmatrix}, \quad C = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix}, \quad D = [0]$$

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

KVL & KCL Equations:

  • Loop-il KVL: $v_{in}(t) = R i_L + L \frac{di_L}{dt} + v_C \implies \frac{di_L}{dt} = -\frac{R}{L}i_L - \frac{1}{L}v_C + \frac{1}{L}v_{in}$.
  • Capacitor current relation: $i_C = C \frac{dv_C}{dt} = i_L \implies \frac{dv_C}{dt} = \frac{1}{C}i_L$.
  • Matrix form: $A = \begin{bmatrix} -R/L & -1/L \\ 1/C & 0 \end{bmatrix}$, $B = \begin{bmatrix} 1/L \\ 0 \end{bmatrix}$, $C = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix}$ (output $v_C$ aaneel).
📝 Exact English Text for University Examination:

1. Identification of State Variables & Inputs:

  • Consider a series RLC circuit excited by an applied voltage source $v(t)$.
  • The independent energy-storing elements are:
    • Inductor $L$: Stores magnetic energy $E_L = \frac{1}{2} L i_L^2 \implies$ State variable $x_1(t) = i_L(t)$.
    • Capacitor $C$: Stores electric energy $E_C = \frac{1}{2} C v_C^2 \implies$ State variable $x_2(t) = v_C(t)$.
  • Input: $u(t) = v(t)$ (source voltage).
  • Output: Let output $y(t) = v_C(t)$ (voltage across capacitor).

2. Derivation of State Equations:

Equation 1 (Using KVL around the series loop):
$$v(t) = v_R(t) + v_L(t) + v_C(t)$$ Using component relationships $v_R = R i_L$ and $v_L = L \frac{d i_L}{dt}$: $$L \frac{d i_L(t)}{dt} + R i_L(t) + v_C(t) = v(t)$$ Rearranging for $\frac{d i_L}{dt} = \dot{x}_1$: $$\frac{d i_L}{dt} = -\frac{R}{L} i_L - \frac{1}{L} v_C + \frac{1}{L} v(t)$$ $$\dot{x}_1(t) = -\frac{R}{L} x_1(t) - \frac{1}{L} x_2(t) + \frac{1}{L} u(t) \quad \text{--- (1)}$$

Equation 2 (Using Capacitor Current Relation):
Because elements are in series, current through capacitor is $i_L$: $$i_C(t) = C \frac{d v_C(t)}{dt} = i_L(t)$$ Rearranging for $\frac{d v_C}{dt} = \dot{x}_2$: $$\dot{x}_2(t) = \frac{1}{C} x_1(t) \quad \text{--- (2)}$$

3. Final Vector-Matrix State-Space Model:
Combining Equations (1) and (2) into $\dot{\mathbf{x}} = A\mathbf{x} + B\mathbf{u}$: $$\begin{bmatrix} \dot{x}_1(t) \\ \dot{x}_2(t) \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} -\frac{R}{L} & -\frac{1}{L} \\ \frac{1}{C} & 0 \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1(t) \\ x_2(t) \end{bmatrix} + \begin{bmatrix} \frac{1}{L} \\ 0 \end{bmatrix} u(t)$$ Output equation for $y(t) = v_C(t) = x_2(t)$: $$y(t) = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1(t) \\ x_2(t) \end{bmatrix} + [0] u(t)$$ Here: $$A = \begin{bmatrix} -R/L & -1/L \\ 1/C & 0 \end{bmatrix}, \quad B = \begin{bmatrix} 1/L \\ 0 \end{bmatrix}, \quad C = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix}, \quad D = [0]$$

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പ്രാദേശിക പഠന സഹായി

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പഠന സഹായി

KVL സമവാക്യം എഴുതി $\frac{di_L}{dt}$-ഉം, കപ്പാസിറ്റർ സമവാക്യത്തിൽ നിന്ന് $\frac{dv_C}{dt}$-ഉം കണ്ടെത്തി മാട്രിക്സ് രൂപത്തിലാക്കുക.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

1. Identification of State Variables & Inputs:

  • Consider a series RLC circuit excited by an applied voltage source $v(t)$.
  • The independent energy-storing elements are:
    • Inductor $L$: Stores magnetic energy $E_L = \frac{1}{2} L i_L^2 \implies$ State variable $x_1(t) = i_L(t)$.
    • Capacitor $C$: Stores electric energy $E_C = \frac{1}{2} C v_C^2 \implies$ State variable $x_2(t) = v_C(t)$.
  • Input: $u(t) = v(t)$ (source voltage).
  • Output: Let output $y(t) = v_C(t)$ (voltage across capacitor).

$$v(t) = v_R(t) + v_L(t) + v_C(t)$$ Using component relationships $v_R = R i_L$ and $v_L = L \frac{d i_L}{dt}$: $$L \frac{d i_L(t)}{dt} + R i_L(t) + v_C(t) = v(t)$$ Rearranging for $\frac{d i_L}{dt} = \dot{x}_1$: $$\frac{d i_L}{dt} = -\frac{R}{L} i_L - \frac{1}{L} v_C + \frac{1}{L} v(t)$$ $$\dot{x}_1(t) = -\frac{R}{L} x_1(t) - \frac{1}{L} x_2(t) + \frac{1}{L} u(t) \quad \text{--- (1)}$$

Equation 2 (Using Capacitor Current Relation):

3. Final Vector-Matrix State-Space Model:
Combining Equations (1) and (2) into $\dot{\mathbf{x}} = A\mathbf{x} + B\mathbf{u}$: $$\begin{bmatrix} \dot{x}_1(t) \\ \dot{x}_2(t) \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} -\frac{R}{L} & -\frac{1}{L} \\ \frac{1}{C} & 0 \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1(t) \\ x_2(t) \end{bmatrix} + \begin{bmatrix} \frac{1}{L} \\ 0 \end{bmatrix} u(t)$$ Output equation for $y(t) = v_C(t) = x_2(t)$: $$y(t) = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1(t) \\ x_2(t) \end{bmatrix} + [0] u(t)$$ Here: $$A = \begin{bmatrix} -R/L & -1/L \\ 1/C & 0 \end{bmatrix}, \quad B = \begin{bmatrix} 1/L \\ 0 \end{bmatrix}, \quad C = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix}, \quad D = [0]$$

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

An electrical circuit is like a water system: the inductor is like a heavy water wheel that keeps spinning due to inertia (current $i_L$), and the capacitor is like an elastic tank that stores water pressure (voltage $v_C$). To know the state of the circuit at any second, you only need to know how fast the wheel is turning ($i_L$) and how pressurized the tank is ($v_C$).

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

Circuit-le battery off aakkiyaalum, inductor-il magnetic field aayum capacitor-il electric charge aayum energy baaki nilkkum. Ee rendu energy-ne kurikkunna variables aanu $i_L$-um $v_C$-um. Avaye states aayi eduthaal circuit-te complete katha parayan pattum!

🇮🇳 മലയാളം ▼

വൈദ്യുതി നിലച്ചാലും ഇൻഡക്ടറിലും കപ്പാസിറ്ററിലും സൂക്ഷിച്ചിരിക്കുന്ന ഊർജ്ജമാണ് സർക്യൂട്ടിന്റെ അവസ്ഥ നിർണ്ണയിക്കുന്നത്!

7/14 Marks (Long Essay / Derivation)
Long Derivation ID: ACS-Q6

Derive the electromechanical state-space model of an armature-controlled DC servomotor with necessary differential equations and block diagram. Armature-Controlled DC motor-te state space equations engane derive cheyyam? ആർമേച്ചർ നിയന്ത്രിത DC മോട്ടോറിന്റെ സ്റ്റേറ്റ് സ്പേസ് സമവാക്യങ്ങൾ രൂപീകരിക്കുക

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$\begin{bmatrix} \dot{i}_a \\ \dot{\omega}_m \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} -R_a/L_a & -K_b/L_a \\ K_t/J & -B/J \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} i_a \\ \omega_m \end{bmatrix} + \begin{bmatrix} 1/L_a \\ 0 \end{bmatrix} v_a(t)$$

Armature current $i_a$ and rotor speed $\omega_m$ form the electromechanical state variables.

📌 ⚡ DC Motor 3x3 State Model
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\begin{bmatrix} \dot{i}_a \\ \dot{\omega} \\ \dot{\theta} \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} -R_a/L_a & -K_b/L_a & 0 \\ K_t/J & -B/J & 0 \\ 0 & 1 & 0 \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} i_a \\ \omega \\ \theta \end{bmatrix} + \begin{bmatrix} 1/L_a \\ 0 \\ 0 \end{bmatrix} v_a$$

Ormavekkan: 3 states: Armature current $i_a$, rotor speed $\omega$, angular position $\theta$. Torque $T = K_t i_a$, Back-EMF $e_b = K_b \omega$.

⚡ ⚡ മോട്ടോർ സ്റ്റേറ്റ് സമവാക്യം
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\dot{\mathbf{x}} = \begin{bmatrix} -R_a/L_a & -K_b/L_a & 0 \\ K_t/J & -B/J & 0 \\ 0 & 1 & 0 \end{bmatrix}\mathbf{x} + \begin{bmatrix} 1/L_a \\ 0 \\ 0 \end{bmatrix}v_a$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: ഇലക്ട്രിക്കൽ ആർമേച്ചർ സമവാക്യവും മെക്കാനിക്കൽ റോട്ടർ സമവാക്യവും ബാക്ക്-ഇഎംഎഫ് വഴി പരസ്പരം ബന്ധപ്പെട്ടിരിക്കുന്നു.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. System Description & Variables:

  • In an armature-controlled DC motor, field current $I_f$ is kept constant, so field flux $\Phi$ is constant.
  • Input: Armature voltage $u(t) = v_a(t)$.
  • State Variables:
    • Electrical state: Armature current $x_1(t) = i_a(t)$.
    • Mechanical state: Rotor angular speed $x_2(t) = \omega_m(t)$ (or position $\theta$).
  • Output: Rotor speed $y(t) = \omega_m(t)$.

2. Governing Differential Equations:

Electrical Equation (Armature Loop KVL):
$$v_a(t) = R_a i_a(t) + L_a \frac{d i_a(t)}{dt} + e_b(t)$$ where back-EMF is proportional to speed: $e_b(t) = K_b \omega_m(t) = K_b x_2(t)$.
Rearranging for $\dot{i}_a = \dot{x}_1$: $$\dot{x}_1(t) = -\frac{R_a}{L_a} x_1(t) - \frac{K_b}{L_a} x_2(t) + \frac{1}{L_a} u(t) \quad \text{--- (1)}$$

Mechanical Equation (Torque Balance):
Developed electromagnetic torque: $T(t) = K_t i_a(t) = K_t x_1(t)$.
Newton's second law for rotor dynamics: $$T(t) = J \frac{d\omega_m(t)}{dt} + B \omega_m(t) + T_L$$ Assuming load torque $T_L = 0$ (unloaded motor): $$J \frac{d\omega_m}{dt} + B \omega_m = K_t i_a$$ Rearranging for $\dot{\omega}_m = \dot{x}_2$: $$\dot{x}_2(t) = \frac{K_t}{J} x_1(t) - \frac{B}{J} x_2(t) \quad \text{--- (2)}$$

3. Matrix State-Space Formulation:
Combining Equations (1) and (2): $$\begin{bmatrix} \dot{x}_1 \\ \dot{x}_2 \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} -\frac{R_a}{L_a} & -\frac{K_b}{L_a} \\ \frac{K_t}{J} & -\frac{B}{J} \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1 \\ x_2 \end{bmatrix} + \begin{bmatrix} \frac{1}{L_a} \\ 0 \end{bmatrix} u(t)$$ Output equation: $$y(t) = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1 \\ x_2 \end{bmatrix}$$ where $J$ is rotor moment of inertia ($\text{kg}\cdot\text{m}^2$), $B$ is viscous friction coefficient ($\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\cdot\text{s/rad}$), $R_a$ is armature resistance ($\Omega$), $L_a$ is armature inductance ($\text{H}$), $K_t$ is torque constant ($\text{N}\cdot\text{m/A}$), and $K_b$ is back-EMF constant ($\text{V}\cdot\text{s/rad}$).

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

Two Coupled Domains:

  1. Electrical: $v_a = R_a i_a + L_a \frac{di_a}{dt} + e_b \implies \frac{di_a}{dt} = -\frac{R_a}{L_a}i_a - \frac{K_b}{L_a}\omega + \frac{1}{L_a}v_a$.
  2. Mechanical: $T = J \frac{d\omega}{dt} + B\omega \implies \frac{d\omega}{dt} = \frac{K_t}{J}i_a - \frac{B}{J}\omega$.
  3. Position kinematics: $\frac{d\theta}{dt} = \omega$.
📝 Exact English Text for University Examination:

1. System Description & Variables:

  • In an armature-controlled DC motor, field current $I_f$ is kept constant, so field flux $\Phi$ is constant.
  • Input: Armature voltage $u(t) = v_a(t)$.
  • State Variables:
    • Electrical state: Armature current $x_1(t) = i_a(t)$.
    • Mechanical state: Rotor angular speed $x_2(t) = \omega_m(t)$ (or position $\theta$).
  • Output: Rotor speed $y(t) = \omega_m(t)$.

2. Governing Differential Equations:

Electrical Equation (Armature Loop KVL):
$$v_a(t) = R_a i_a(t) + L_a \frac{d i_a(t)}{dt} + e_b(t)$$ where back-EMF is proportional to speed: $e_b(t) = K_b \omega_m(t) = K_b x_2(t)$.
Rearranging for $\dot{i}_a = \dot{x}_1$: $$\dot{x}_1(t) = -\frac{R_a}{L_a} x_1(t) - \frac{K_b}{L_a} x_2(t) + \frac{1}{L_a} u(t) \quad \text{--- (1)}$$

Mechanical Equation (Torque Balance):
Developed electromagnetic torque: $T(t) = K_t i_a(t) = K_t x_1(t)$.
Newton's second law for rotor dynamics: $$T(t) = J \frac{d\omega_m(t)}{dt} + B \omega_m(t) + T_L$$ Assuming load torque $T_L = 0$ (unloaded motor): $$J \frac{d\omega_m}{dt} + B \omega_m = K_t i_a$$ Rearranging for $\dot{\omega}_m = \dot{x}_2$: $$\dot{x}_2(t) = \frac{K_t}{J} x_1(t) - \frac{B}{J} x_2(t) \quad \text{--- (2)}$$

3. Matrix State-Space Formulation:
Combining Equations (1) and (2): $$\begin{bmatrix} \dot{x}_1 \\ \dot{x}_2 \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} -\frac{R_a}{L_a} & -\frac{K_b}{L_a} \\ \frac{K_t}{J} & -\frac{B}{J} \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1 \\ x_2 \end{bmatrix} + \begin{bmatrix} \frac{1}{L_a} \\ 0 \end{bmatrix} u(t)$$ Output equation: $$y(t) = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1 \\ x_2 \end{bmatrix}$$ where $J$ is rotor moment of inertia ($\text{kg}\cdot\text{m}^2$), $B$ is viscous friction coefficient ($\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\cdot\text{s/rad}$), $R_a$ is armature resistance ($\Omega$), $L_a$ is armature inductance ($\text{H}$), $K_t$ is torque constant ($\text{N}\cdot\text{m/A}$), and $K_b$ is back-EMF constant ($\text{V}\cdot\text{s/rad}$).

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പ്രാദേശിക പഠന സഹായി

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പഠന സഹായി

ആർമേച്ചർ ലൂപ്പിൽ KVL ഉപയോഗിച്ച് $\frac{di_a}{dt}$-ഉം, ന്യൂട്ടന്റെ റൊട്ടേഷണൽ നിയമം ഉപയോഗിച്ച് $\frac{d\omega}{dt}$-ഉം കണ്ടെത്തുക.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

1. System Description & Variables:

  • In an armature-controlled DC motor, field current $I_f$ is kept constant, so field flux $\Phi$ is constant.
  • Input: Armature voltage $u(t) = v_a(t)$.
  • State Variables:
    • Electrical state: Armature current $x_1(t) = i_a(t)$.
    • Mechanical state: Rotor angular speed $x_2(t) = \omega_m(t)$ (or position $\theta$).
  • Output: Rotor speed $y(t) = \omega_m(t)$.

2. Governing Differential Equations:

Electrical Equation (Armature Loop KVL):
$$v_a(t) = R_a i_a(t) + L_a \frac{d i_a(t)}{dt} + e_b(t)$$ where back-EMF is proportional to speed: $e_b(t) = K_b \omega_m(t) = K_b x_2(t)$.
Rearranging for $\dot{i}_a = \dot{x}_1$: $$\dot{x}_1(t) = -\frac{R_a}{L_a} x_1(t) - \frac{K_b}{L_a} x_2(t) + \frac{1}{L_a} u(t) \quad \text{--- (1)}$$

Mechanical Equation (Torque Balance):

Newton's second law for rotor dynamics: $$T(t) = J \frac{d\omega_m(t)}{dt} + B \omega_m(t) + T_L$$ Assuming load torque $T_L = 0$ (unloaded motor): $$J \frac{d\omega_m}{dt} + B \omega_m = K_t i_a$$ Rearranging for $\dot{\omega}_m = \dot{x}_2$: $$\dot{x}_2(t) = \frac{K_t}{J} x_1(t) - \frac{B}{J} x_2(t) \quad \text{--- (2)}$$

3. Matrix State-Space Formulation:
Combining Equations (1) and (2): $$\begin{bmatrix} \dot{x}_1 \\ \dot{x}_2 \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} -\frac{R_a}{L_a} & -\frac{K_b}{L_a} \\ \frac{K_t}{J} & -\frac{B}{J} \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1 \\ x_2 \end{bmatrix} + \begin{bmatrix} \frac{1}{L_a} \\ 0 \end{bmatrix} u(t)$$ Output equation: $$y(t) = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1 \\ x_2 \end{bmatrix}$$ where $J$ is rotor moment of inertia ($\text{kg}\cdot\text{m}^2$), $B$ is viscous friction coefficient ($\text{N}\cdot\text{m}\cdot\text{s/rad}$), $R_a$ is armature resistance ($\Omega$), $L_a$ is armature inductance ($\text{H}$), $K_t$ is torque constant ($\text{N}\cdot\text{m/A}$), and $K_b$ is back-EMF constant ($\text{V}\cdot\text{s/rad}$).

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

A DC motor is part electrical and part mechanical. The electrical part takes in voltage and pushes armature current $i_a$ against coil resistance and back-EMF. That current creates magnetic torque, which spins the mechanical rotor inertia $J$ up to speed $\omega_m$. So the motor has two states: electrical current $i_a$ and mechanical speed $\omega_m$.

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

Electrical wire-il koodi ozhukunna current motor-te shaft-ine mechanical motion aakki thirikkunnu. Thiriyumbol motor thaniye oru generator pole act cheythu back-EMF $e_b$ undakki current-ine oppose cheyyunnu. Ee electro-mechanical interaction aanu motor equations!

💡 ലളിതമായി മനസ്സിലാക്കാം — പ്രാദേശിക ഉദാഹരണം
🇮🇳 മലയാളം ▼

കറന്റ് കൊടുക്കുമ്പോൾ മോട്ടോർ കറങ്ങുന്നു, കറങ്ങുമ്പോൾ തിരിച്ച് ബാക്ക്-ഇഎംഎഫ് ഉണ്ടാകുന്നു. ഇവ തമ്മിലുള്ള സംയോജനമാണ് ഡിസി മോട്ടോർ മോഡൽ!

7/14 Marks (Long Essay / Derivation)
Long Derivation ID: ACS-Q7

Derive the controllable canonical form and diagonal (Jordan) canonical form from a given system transfer function. Controllable Canonical Form-um Diagonal (Jordan) Canonical Form-um engane derive cheyyam? കൺട്രോളബിൾ കാനോനിക്കൽ ഫോമും ഡയഗണൽ (ജോർദാൻ) ഫോമും എങ്ങനെ കണ്ടുപിടിക്കാം?

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$A_{\text{controllable}} = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 1 & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & 1 \\ -a_0 & -a_1 & -a_2 \end{bmatrix}, \quad A_{\text{diagonal}} = \begin{bmatrix} \lambda_1 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & \lambda_2 & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & \lambda_3 \end{bmatrix}$$

Canonical forms standardize state space for easy controllability, observability, and pole placement.

📌 ⚡ Canonical Forms Comparison
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\Lambda = M^{-1}AM = \operatorname{diag}(\lambda_1, \lambda_2, \dots, \lambda_n), \quad \dot{\mathbf{z}} = \Lambda \mathbf{z} + M^{-1}B u$$

Ormavekkan: Controllable canonical form pole-placement eluppamaakkunnu. Diagonal form modes-ine decouple cheythu oro mode-um independently solve cheyyan sahayikkunnu (using modal matrix $M$ of eigenvectors).

📌 ⚡ ഡയഗണൽ മാട്രിക്സ്
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\Lambda = \operatorname{diag}(\lambda_1, \lambda_2, \dots, \lambda_n) = M^{-1}AM$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: ഐഗൻവെക്ടറുകളുടെ മോഡൽ മാട്രിക്സ് ഉപയോഗിച്ച് സിസ്റ്റം വേരിയബിളുകളെ പരസ്പരം വേർപെടുത്താം (Decoupling).

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Introduction:
Canonical forms are standardized state-space representations derived from transfer functions that highlight specific system properties (controllability, observability, or natural modes).

Consider a general 3rd-order transfer function: $$G(s) = \frac{Y(s)}{U(s)} = \frac{b_2 s^2 + b_1 s + b_0}{s^3 + a_2 s^2 + a_1 s + a_0}$$

2. Controllable Canonical Form (CCF):

  • Directly obtained from phase-variable realization: $$\begin{bmatrix} \dot{x}_1 \\ \dot{x}_2 \\ \dot{x}_3 \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 1 & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & 1 \\ -a_0 & -a_1 & -a_2 \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1 \\ x_2 \\ x_3 \end{bmatrix} + \begin{bmatrix} 0 \\ 0 \\ 1 \end{bmatrix} u(t)$$ $$y(t) = \begin{bmatrix} b_0 & b_1 & b_2 \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1 \\ x_2 \\ x_3 \end{bmatrix}$$
  • Significance: Controllability matrix $Q_c = [B \ AB \ A^2B]$ always has full rank ($3$), guaranteeing that the system is controllable. Ideal for pole placement controller design.

3. Diagonal Canonical Form (Jordan Canonical Form):

  • Obtained by partial fraction expansion of $G(s)$ when poles $\lambda_1, \lambda_2, \lambda_3$ are distinct: $$G(s) = \frac{c_1}{s - \lambda_1} + \frac{c_2}{s - \lambda_2} + \frac{c_3}{s - \lambda_3}$$
  • Assign state variables to each decoupled pole mode: $X_i(s) = \frac{1}{s - \lambda_i} U(s) \implies \dot{x}_i = \lambda_i x_i + u(t)$.
  • Matrix Representation: $$\begin{bmatrix} \dot{x}_1 \\ \dot{x}_2 \\ \dot{x}_3 \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} \lambda_1 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & \lambda_2 & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & \lambda_3 \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1 \\ x_2 \\ x_3 \end{bmatrix} + \begin{bmatrix} 1 \\ 1 \\ 1 \end{bmatrix} u(t)$$ $$y(t) = \begin{bmatrix} c_1 & c_2 & c_3 \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1 \\ x_2 \\ x_3 \end{bmatrix}$$
  • Significance: Matrix $A$ is completely uncoupled (diagonal). Controllability and observability can be inspected directly: if any element in $B$ is zero, that mode is uncontrollable; if any element in $C$ is zero, that mode is unobservable.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

1. Controllable Form: Companion matrix format. Last row gives $-a_0, -a_1, \dots, -a_{n-1}$. Matrix $B = [0, 0, \dots, 1]^T$. Direct state feedback $u = -Kx$ calculate cheyyan ithaanu best.

2. Diagonal Form: Matrix $A$-yude eigenvectors $v_1, v_2, \dots, v_n$ eduthu modal matrix $M = [v_1 \dots v_n]$ undakkuka. Athu vechu transform cheythaal $\Lambda = M^{-1}AM = \operatorname{diag}(\lambda_1, \dots, \lambda_n)$. Coupling full maaripoyitt oro first-order equation separate aayi kitum!

📝 Exact English Text for University Examination:

1. Introduction:
Canonical forms are standardized state-space representations derived from transfer functions that highlight specific system properties (controllability, observability, or natural modes).

Consider a general 3rd-order transfer function: $$G(s) = \frac{Y(s)}{U(s)} = \frac{b_2 s^2 + b_1 s + b_0}{s^3 + a_2 s^2 + a_1 s + a_0}$$

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പഠന സഹായി

കൺട്രോളബിൾ ഫോം പോൾ പ്ലേസ്‌മെന്റിനും, ഡയഗണൽ ഫോം ഓരോ മോഡുകളെയും വെവ്വേറെ വിശകലനം ചെയ്യാനും ഉപയോഗിക്കുന്നു.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

If three workers are working together on a car assembly line, Controllable Form is like an assembly line where worker 1 passes parts to worker 2, who passes to worker 3. Diagonal Form is like giving each worker their own independent table where they work in parallel without touching each other's parts. Both build the same car, but Diagonal Form lets you immediately spot which worker is slacking off!

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

Oru complex dance troupe alochikku: Ellaavarum koottikuzhanju kalichaal aaraanu thettu cheyyunnathennu ariyilla (Coupled system). Pakshe ellaavarkkum separate rows koduthu mathram nirthiyaal (Diagonalization), oro aaludeyum performance independently evaluate cheyyam!

💡 ലളിതമായി മനസ്സിലാക്കാം — പ്രാദേശിക ഉദാഹരണം
🇮🇳 മലയാളം ▼

കെട്ടുപിണഞ്ഞു കിടക്കുന്ന നൂലുകളെ നേരെയാക്കി വെവ്വേറെയാക്കുന്നത് പോലെയാണ് ഡയഗണലൈസേഷൻ!

7/14 Marks (Long Essay / Derivation)
Long Derivation ID: ACS-Q7B

Derive the state space equations for a field-controlled DC motor and obtain its overall transfer function. Field-Controlled DC Servomotor-te State-Space Model engane undakkam? ഫീൽഡ് നിയന്ത്രിത DC സെർവോമോട്ടോറിന്റെ സ്റ്റേറ്റ് സ്പേസ് മോഡൽ രൂപീകരിക്കുക

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$\begin{bmatrix} \dot{i}_f \\ \dot{\omega}_m \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} -R_f/L_f & 0 \\ K_{tf}/J & -B/J \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} i_f \\ \omega_m \end{bmatrix} + \begin{bmatrix} 1/L_f \\ 0 \end{bmatrix} v_f(t)$$

Armature current $I_a$ is constant; control input is applied to the field winding $v_f(t)$.

📌 ⚡ Field-Controlled 2x2 Model
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\begin{bmatrix} \frac{di_f}{dt} \\ \frac{d\omega}{dt} \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} -R_f/L_f & 0 \\ K_{tf}/J & -B/J \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} i_f \\ \omega \end{bmatrix} + \begin{bmatrix} 1/L_f \\ 0 \end{bmatrix} v_f$$

Ormavekkan: Armature current $I_a$ constant aanu. So back-EMF feedback loop-e illa! Field circuit-il ninnu mechanical rotor-lekku matram unidirectional drive aanu.

📌 ⚡ ഫീൽഡ് മോഡൽ മാട്രിക്സ്
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\dot{\mathbf{x}} = \begin{bmatrix} -R_f/L_f & 0 \\ K_{tf}/J & -B/J \end{bmatrix}\mathbf{x} + \begin{bmatrix} 1/L_f \\ 0 \end{bmatrix}v_f$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: ആർമേച്ചർ കറന്റ് സ്ഥിരമായതിനാൽ ബാക്ക്-ഇഎംഎഫ് ഫീഡ്‌ബാക്ക് ഉണ്ടാകുന്നില്ല.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Operating Principle:

  • In a field-controlled DC motor, the armature current $I_a$ is supplied by a constant current source.
  • The control input voltage $u(t) = v_f(t)$ is applied to the field circuit.
  • Torque developed is proportional to field current: $T(t) = K_f I_a i_f(t) = K_{tf} i_f(t)$.
  • Advantage: Field winding requires much less power than the heavy armature circuit.

2. Governing Differential Equations:

Field Circuit KVL:
$$v_f(t) = R_f i_f(t) + L_f \frac{d i_f(t)}{dt}$$ where $R_f$ is field winding resistance and $L_f$ is field inductance.
Rearranging for $\dot{i}_f$: $$\frac{d i_f}{dt} = -\frac{R_f}{L_f} i_f(t) + \frac{1}{L_f} v_f(t) \quad \text{--- (1)}$$

Mechanical Torque Balance Equation:
$$T(t) = J \frac{d\omega_m(t)}{dt} + B\omega_m(t)$$ Substituting $T(t) = K_{tf} i_f(t)$: $$J \frac{d\omega_m}{dt} + B\omega_m = K_{tf} i_f$$ Rearranging for $\dot{\omega}_m$: $$\frac{d\omega_m}{dt} = \frac{K_{tf}}{J} i_f(t) - \frac{B}{J} \omega_m(t) \quad \text{--- (2)}$$

3. Matrix State-Space Formulation:
Define states $x_1(t) = i_f(t)$, $x_2(t) = \omega_m(t)$, input $u(t) = v_f(t)$: $$\begin{bmatrix} \dot{x}_1 \\ \dot{x}_2 \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} -\frac{R_f}{L_f} & 0 \\ \frac{K_{tf}}{J} & -\frac{B}{J} \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1 \\ x_2 \end{bmatrix} + \begin{bmatrix} \frac{1}{L_f} \\ 0 \end{bmatrix} u(t)$$ Output equation for rotor speed: $$y(t) = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_1 \\ x_2 \end{bmatrix}$$ Key Difference from Armature Control: Notice $A_{12} = 0$ because there is no back-EMF term in the field circuit! The field dynamics are completely independent of the mechanical speed.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

1. Field circuit: $v_f = R_f i_f + L_f \frac{di_f}{dt} \implies \frac{di_f}{dt} = -\frac{R_f}{L_f}i_f + \frac{1}{L_f}v_f$.

2. Torque: $T_e = K_f \phi I_a = K_{tf} i_f = J \dot{\omega} + B\omega \implies \dot{\omega} = \frac{K_{tf}}{J}i_f - \frac{B}{J}\omega$.

Matrix $A$-yude top-right element $0$ aanu, because motor speed field current-ine thirichu affect cheyyilla!

  • In a field-controlled DC motor, the armature current $I_a$ is supplied by a constant current source.
  • The control input voltage $u(t) = v_f(t)$ is applied to the field circuit.
  • Torque developed is proportional to field current: $T(t) = K_f I_a i_f(t) = K_{tf} i_f(t)$.

2. Governing Differential Equations:

$$v_f(t) = R_f i_f(t) + L_f \frac{d i_f(t)}{dt}$$ where $R_f$ is field winding resistance and $L_f$ is field inductance.

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പഠന സഹായി

ഫീൽഡ് വോൾട്ടേജ് സമവാക്യവും റോട്ടർ ടോർക്ക് സമവാക്യവും എഴുതുക. ബാക്ക്-ഇഎംഎഫ് ഇല്ലാത്തതിനാൽ മാട്രിക്സ് ലളിതമാണ്.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

1. Operating Principle:

  • In a field-controlled DC motor, the armature current $I_a$ is supplied by a constant current source.
  • The control input voltage $u(t) = v_f(t)$ is applied to the field circuit.
  • Torque developed is proportional to field current: $T(t) = K_f I_a i_f(t) = K_{tf} i_f(t)$.
  • Advantage: Field winding requires much less power than the heavy armature circuit.

2. Governing Differential Equations:

Field Circuit KVL:
$$v_f(t) = R_f i_f(t) + L_f \frac{d i_f(t)}{dt}$$ where $R_f$ is field winding resistance and $L_f$ is field inductance.
Rearranging for $\dot{i}_f$: $$\frac{d i_f}{dt} = -\frac{R_f}{L_f} i_f(t) + \frac{1}{L_f} v_f(t) \quad \text{--- (1)}$$

Mechanical Torque Balance Equation:
$$T(t) = J \frac{d\omega_m(t)}{dt} + B\omega_m(t)$$ Substituting $T(t) = K_{tf} i_f(t)$: $$J \frac{d\omega_m}{dt} + B\omega_m = K_{tf} i_f$$ Rearranging for $\dot{\omega}_m$: $$\frac{d\omega_m}{dt} = \frac{K_{tf}}{J} i_f(t) - \frac{B}{J} \omega_m(t) \quad \text{--- (2)}$$

3. Matrix State-Space Formulation:

The field dynamics are completely independent of the mechanical speed.

Figure: Figure 1.7B: Electromechanical Schematic and State Flow of Field-Controlled DC Motor

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

In field control, you leave the main motor power running constant and only adjust the small magnetic coil on the side. It's like driving a car with the gas pedal floored, but controlling your speed by turning an electromagnet that pulls on the clutch. Because field coils are small, you need only tiny control signals to run the motor!

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

Armature control-il motor thiriyumbol back-EMF undaayi circuit-ine thirichu distrub cheyyum. Pakshe field control-il armature current constant aayathukond back-EMF feedback illa — ithu oru one-way street pole aanu!
