ഇവിടെ ഇൻപുട്ട് കൺട്രോൾ സിഗ്നൽ ഒരു വൺ-വേ ട്രാഫിക് പോലെയാണ്, മോട്ടോർ സ്പീഡ് സർക്യൂട്ടിനെ തിരിച്ചൊന്നും ബാധിക്കില്ല!

Module 2

Solution of State Equations, State Transition Matrix & Discrete Systems

5 Elaborated University Questions & Answers

3 Marks (Short Answer)
Short Answer ID: ACS-Q8

Derive the solution of homogeneous (autonomous) continuous-time state equations using the Laplace transform method. Autonomous state equation $\dot{\mathbf{x}} = A\mathbf{x}$-nte solution engane derive cheyyam? ഹോമോജീനിയസ് സ്റ്റേറ്റ് സമവാക്യത്തിന്റെ പരിഹാരം എങ്ങനെ കണ്ടെത്താം?

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = A\mathbf{x}(t) \implies \mathbf{x}(t) = \mathcal{L}^{-1}\{(sI - A)^{-1}\}\mathbf{x}(0) = e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0)$$

Unforced solution depends purely on initial state energy $\mathbf{x}(0)$ modulated by transition matrix $\Phi(t)$.

📌 ⚡ Autonomous Solution Formula
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\mathbf{x}(t) = \mathcal{L}^{-1}\{(sI - A)^{-1}\} \mathbf{x}(0) = e^{At} \mathbf{x}(0) = \Phi(t) \mathbf{x}(0)$$

Ormavekkan: External input illatha samayathu ($u(t) = 0$), initial energy $\mathbf{x}(0)$ karanam undakunna response aanu Zero-Input Response (ZIR). Resolvent matrix $(sI - A)^{-1}$-te inverse Laplace eduthal State Transition Matrix $\Phi(t)$ kitum.

⚡ ⚡ ZIR സമവാക്യം
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\mathbf{x}(t) = e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0) = \Phi(t)\mathbf{x}(0)$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: പുറത്തുനിന്ന് ഇൻപുട്ട് ഇല്ലാതെ പ്രാരംഭ ഊർജ്ജം കൊണ്ടുമാത്രം ഉണ്ടാകുന്ന പ്രതികരണമാണിത്.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Problem Statement:
An unforced (homogeneous/autonomous) LTI continuous system has zero input ($\mathbf{u}(t) = \mathbf{0}$) and non-zero initial conditions $\mathbf{x}(0)$: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = A\mathbf{x}(t), \quad t \ge 0$$

2. Step-by-Step Derivation:

  • Take the unilateral Laplace transform on both sides: $$\mathcal{L}\{\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t)\} = s\mathbf{X}(s) - \mathbf{x}(0) = A\mathbf{X}(s)$$
  • Rearrange to group the state transform $\mathbf{X}(s)$: $$s\mathbf{X}(s) - A\mathbf{X}(s) = \mathbf{x}(0)$$ $$(sI - A)\mathbf{X}(s) = \mathbf{x}(0)$$
  • Premultiply both sides by the inverse $(sI - A)^{-1}$: $$\mathbf{X}(s) = (sI - A)^{-1}\mathbf{x}(0)$$
  • Take the inverse Laplace transform: $$\mathbf{x}(t) = \mathcal{L}^{-1}\left\{(sI - A)^{-1}\right\} \mathbf{x}(0)$$

Final Solution: $$\mathbf{x}(t) = e^{At} \mathbf{x}(0) = \Phi(t) \mathbf{x}(0)$$ where $\Phi(t) = e^{At} = \mathcal{L}^{-1}\{(sI - A)^{-1}\}$ is the State Transition Matrix (STM).

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

1. Given: $\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = A\mathbf{x}(t), \quad \mathbf{x}(0) = \mathbf{x}_0$.

2. Laplace transform: $s\mathbf{X}(s) - \mathbf{x}(0) = A\mathbf{X}(s) \implies (sI - A)\mathbf{X}(s) = \mathbf{x}(0)$.

3. $\mathbf{X}(s) = (sI - A)^{-1}\mathbf{x}(0)$.

4. Taking inverse Laplace: $\mathbf{x}(t) = \mathcal{L}^{-1}\{(sI - A)^{-1}\}\mathbf{x}(0) = e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0)$.

📝 Exact English Text for University Examination:

1. Problem Statement:
An unforced (homogeneous/autonomous) LTI continuous system has zero input ($\mathbf{u}(t) = \mathbf{0}$) and non-zero initial conditions $\mathbf{x}(0)$: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = A\mathbf{x}(t), \quad t \ge 0$$

2. Step-by-Step Derivation:

  • Take the unilateral Laplace transform on both sides: $$\mathcal{L}\{\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t)\} = s\mathbf{X}(s) - \mathbf{x}(0) = A\mathbf{X}(s)$$
  • Rearrange to group the state transform $\mathbf{X}(s)$: $$s\mathbf{X}(s) - A\mathbf{X}(s) = \mathbf{x}(0)$$ $$(sI - A)\mathbf{X}(s) = \mathbf{x}(0)$$
  • Premultiply both sides by the inverse $(sI - A)^{-1}$: $$\mathbf{X}(s) = (sI - A)^{-1}\mathbf{x}(0)$$
  • Take the inverse Laplace transform: $$\mathbf{x}(t) = \mathcal{L}^{-1}\left\{(sI - A)^{-1}\right\} \mathbf{x}(0)$$

Final Solution: $$\mathbf{x}(t) = e^{At} \mathbf{x}(0) = \Phi(t) \mathbf{x}(0)$$ where $\Phi(t) = e^{At} = \mathcal{L}^{-1}\{(sI - A)^{-1}\}$ is the State Transition Matrix (STM).

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പ്രാദേശിക പഠന സഹായി

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പഠന സഹായി

ലാപ്ലേസ് ട്രാൻസ്ഫോർം എടുത്ത് $(sI - A)^{-1}$ ന്റെ ഇൻവേഴ്സ് എടുക്കുക.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

1. Problem Statement:
An unforced (homogeneous/autonomous) LTI continuous system has zero input ($\mathbf{u}(t) = \mathbf{0}$) and non-zero initial conditions $\mathbf{x}(0)$: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = A\mathbf{x}(t), \quad t \ge 0$$

2. Step-by-Step Derivation:

  • Take the unilateral Laplace transform on both sides: $$\mathcal{L}\{\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t)\} = s\mathbf{X}(s) - \mathbf{x}(0) = A\mathbf{X}(s)$$
  • Rearrange to group the state transform $\mathbf{X}(s)$: $$s\mathbf{X}(s) - A\mathbf{X}(s) = \mathbf{x}(0)$$ $$(sI - A)\mathbf{X}(s) = \mathbf{x}(0)$$
  • Premultiply both sides by the inverse $(sI - A)^{-1}$: $$\mathbf{X}(s) = (sI - A)^{-1}\mathbf{x}(0)$$
  • Take the inverse Laplace transform: $$\mathbf{x}(t) = \mathcal{L}^{-1}\left\{(sI - A)^{-1}\right\} \mathbf{x}(0)$$

Final Solution: $$\mathbf{x}(t) = e^{At} \mathbf{x}(0) = \Phi(t) \mathbf{x}(0)$$ where $\Phi(t) = e^{At} = \mathcal{L}^{-1}\{(sI - A)^{-1}\}$ is the State Transition Matrix (STM).

Figure: Figure 2.1: Resolvent Matrix and Laplace Transform Inversion Flow

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

Think of plucking a guitar string and letting go ($u=0$). The string vibrates and slowly dies out. How it vibrates depends only on how hard you plucked it initially ($\mathbf{x}(0)$) and the natural stiffness and dampening of the string ($e^{At}$).

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

Oru unjal (swing) thallividatha samayathu, alpam mukalilekku valichu pidichu vittal athu thaniye aadi aadi nilkkunna response aanu Zero-Input Response. Ee aattathinte pattern theerumanikkunnath matrix exponential $e^{At}$ aanu!

💡 ലളിതമായി മനസ്സിലാക്കാം — പ്രാദേശിക ഉദാഹരണം
🇮🇳 മലയാളം ▼

ഒരു ഊഞ്ഞാൽ പുറത്തുനിന്ന് തള്ളാതെ, ആദ്യം ഒരല്പം ഉയർത്തി വിട്ടാൽ സ്വയം ആടുന്നതാണ് സീറോ-ഇൻപുട്ട് റെസ്‌പോൺസ്!

3 Marks (Short Answer)
Short Answer ID: ACS-Q9

Define state transition matrix. State and prove any four important properties of the state transition matrix. State Transition Matrix $\Phi(t) = e^{At}$-nte 5 main properties enthokke aanu? സ്റ്റേറ്റ് ട്രാൻസിഷൻ മാട്രിക്സിന്റെ ($\Phi(t)$) പ്രധാന സവിശേഷതകൾ എന്തൊക്കെയാണ്?

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$\Phi(t) = e^{At} = \sum_{k=0}^{\infty} \frac{A^k t^k}{k!}$$

Core properties: $\Phi(0) = I$, $\Phi(-t) = \Phi^{-1}(t)$, $\Phi(t_1+t_2) = \Phi(t_1)\Phi(t_2)$, $[\Phi(t)]^k = \Phi(kt)$.

📌 ⚡ 5 Core STM Properties
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\Phi(0) = I, \;\; \Phi^{-1}(t) = \Phi(-t), \;\; \Phi(t_1+t_2) = \Phi(t_1)\Phi(t_2), \;\; [\Phi(t)]^k = \Phi(kt), \;\; \dot{\Phi}(t) = A\Phi(t)$$

Ormavekkan: Exam-il ee 5 properties ezhuthi proof kodukkanam: $\Phi(0)=e^0=I$; time reverse cheythaal inverse $\Phi(-t) = \Phi^{-1}(t)$; matrix derivative $\dot{\Phi} = A e^{At} = A\Phi$.

📌 ⚡ 5 പ്രധാന സവിശേഷതകൾ
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\Phi(0) = I, \quad \Phi(-t) = \Phi^{-1}(t), \quad \Phi(t_1+t_2) = \Phi(t_1)\Phi(t_2)$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: സമയം പൂജ്യമാകുമ്പോൾ ഐഡന്റിറ്റി മാട്രിക്സ് ലഭിക്കുന്നു; നെഗറ്റീവ് സമയം മാട്രിക്സിന്റെ ഇൻവേഴ്സ് നൽകുന്നു.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

Definition: The State Transition Matrix $\Phi(t) = e^{At}$ maps the initial state $\mathbf{x}(0)$ to state $\mathbf{x}(t)$ in an unforced continuous system: $\mathbf{x}(t) = \Phi(t)\mathbf{x}(0)$.

Four Important Properties with Proofs:

  1. Identity Property at $t = 0$: $$\Phi(0) = e^{A \cdot 0} = I$$ Proof: At $t = 0$, $\mathbf{x}(0) = \Phi(0)\mathbf{x}(0) \implies \Phi(0) = I$.
  2. Inverse Transition Property: $$\Phi(-t) = [\Phi(t)]^{-1} = e^{-At}$$ Proof: $\Phi(t)\Phi(-t) = e^{At} e^{-At} = e^{A(t - t)} = e^0 = I \implies \Phi(-t) = [\Phi(t)]^{-1}$.
  3. Semi-Group Composition Property: $$\Phi(t_1 + t_2) = \Phi(t_1)\Phi(t_2) = \Phi(t_2)\Phi(t_1)$$ Proof: $\Phi(t_1 + t_2) = e^{A(t_1 + t_2)} = e^{At_1} e^{At_2} = \Phi(t_1)\Phi(t_2)$.
  4. Power Multiplication Property: $$[\Phi(t)]^k = \Phi(kt) \quad \text{for any integer } k$$ Proof: $[e^{At}]^k = e^{A(kt)} = \Phi(kt)$.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide
  • Property 1: $\Phi(0) = e^{A \cdot 0} = I$ (Identity matrix).
  • Property 2: $\Phi^{-1}(t) = [e^{At}]^{-1} = e^{-At} = \Phi(-t)$ (Time reversal gives inverse).
  • Property 3: $\Phi(t_1 + t_2) = e^{A(t_1+t_2)} = e^{At_1} \cdot e^{At_2} = \Phi(t_1)\Phi(t_2)$ (Semigroup property).
  • Property 4: $[\Phi(t)]^k = \Phi(kt)$.
  • Property 5: $\frac{d}{dt}\Phi(t) = \frac{d}{dt}(e^{At}) = A e^{At} = A \Phi(t) = \Phi(t) A$.
📝 Exact English Text for University Examination:

Definition: The State Transition Matrix $\Phi(t) = e^{At}$ maps the initial state $\mathbf{x}(0)$ to state $\mathbf{x}(t)$ in an unforced continuous system: $\mathbf{x}(t) = \Phi(t)\mathbf{x}(0)$.

Four Important Properties with Proofs:

  1. Identity Property at $t = 0$: $$\Phi(0) = e^{A \cdot 0} = I$$ Proof: At $t = 0$, $\mathbf{x}(0) = \Phi(0)\mathbf{x}(0) \implies \Phi(0) = I$.
  2. Inverse Transition Property: $$\Phi(-t) = [\Phi(t)]^{-1} = e^{-At}$$ Proof: $\Phi(t)\Phi(-t) = e^{At} e^{-At} = e^{A(t - t)} = e^0 = I \implies \Phi(-t) = [\Phi(t)]^{-1}$.
  3. Semi-Group Composition Property: $$\Phi(t_1 + t_2) = \Phi(t_1)\Phi(t_2) = \Phi(t_2)\Phi(t_1)$$ Proof: $\Phi(t_1 + t_2) = e^{A(t_1 + t_2)} = e^{At_1} e^{At_2} = \Phi(t_1)\Phi(t_2)$.
  4. Power Multiplication Property: $$[\Phi(t)]^k = \Phi(kt) \quad \text{for any integer } k$$ Proof: $[e^{At}]^k = e^{A(kt)} = \Phi(kt)$.

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പ്രാദേശിക പഠന സഹായി

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പഠന സഹായി

5 പ്രോപ്പർട്ടികളും അവയുടെ ചെറിയ തെളിവുകളും എഴുതുക.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

Definition: The State Transition Matrix $\Phi(t) = e^{At}$ maps the initial state $\mathbf{x}(0)$ to state $\mathbf{x}(t)$ in an unforced continuous system: $\mathbf{x}(t) = \Phi(t)\mathbf{x}(0)$.

Four Important Properties with Proofs:

  1. Identity Property at $t = 0$: $$\Phi(0) = e^{A \cdot 0} = I$$ Proof: At $t = 0$, $\mathbf{x}(0) = \Phi(0)\mathbf{x}(0) \implies \Phi(0) = I$.
  2. Inverse Transition Property: $$\Phi(-t) = [\Phi(t)]^{-1} = e^{-At}$$ Proof: $\Phi(t)\Phi(-t) = e^{At} e^{-At} = e^{A(t - t)} = e^0 = I \implies \Phi(-t) = [\Phi(t)]^{-1}$.
  3. Semi-Group Composition Property: $$\Phi(t_1 + t_2) = \Phi(t_1)\Phi(t_2) = \Phi(t_2)\Phi(t_1)$$ Proof: $\Phi(t_1 + t_2) = e^{A(t_1 + t_2)} = e^{At_1} e^{At_2} = \Phi(t_1)\Phi(t_2)$.
  4. Power Multiplication Property: $$[\Phi(t)]^k = \Phi(kt) \quad \text{for any integer } k$$ Proof: $[e^{At}]^k = e^{A(kt)} = \Phi(kt)$.

Figure: Figure 2.2: Composition and Inversion Geometry of State Transition Matrix

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

The state transition matrix is a time machine for dynamic systems: $\Phi(1)$ tells you where the system will be in 1 second. Moving forward 2 seconds ($\Phi(2)$) is the exact same as moving 1 second twice ($\Phi(1) \cdot \Phi(1)$). And rewinding 1 second in time is simply the mathematical inverse matrix ($\Phi(-1) = \Phi(1)^{-1}$).

STM oru time machine pole aanu: $\Phi(t_1)$ vechu time $t_1$-lekku travel cheyyam, pinne $\Phi(t_2)$ vechu $t_2$-lekku travel cheyyam — athu direct aayi $\Phi(t_1 + t_2)$ travel cheyyunnathinu equal aanu! Negative time $-\Phi(-t)$ travel cheythal thirichu start cheytha sthalathu thanne ethum!

3 Marks (Short Answer)
Short Answer ID: ACS-Q9B

Derive the pulse transfer function and discrete state transition solution for a discrete-time LTI state model with Zero-Order Hold (ZOH). Discrete-Time State Space model-um Pulse Transfer Function-um engane define cheyyam? ഡിസ്ക്രീറ്റ്-ടൈം സ്റ്റേറ്റ് സ്പേസ് മോഡലും പൾസ് ട്രാൻസ്ഫർ ഫങ്ഷനും വിശദീകരിക്കുക

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$\mathbf{x}(k+1) = G\mathbf{x}(k) + H\mathbf{u}(k), \quad G(z) = C(zI - G)^{-1}H + D$$

Discrete state equations use difference equations; $z$-transform replaces Laplace transform.

⚡ ⚡ Discrete State Equations & Pulse TF
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\mathbf{x}(k+1) = G\mathbf{x}(k) + H\mathbf{u}(k), \quad \mathbf{y}(k) = C\mathbf{x}(k) + D\mathbf{u}(k), \quad G(z) = C(zI - G)^{-1}H + D$$

Ormavekkan: Continuous time-le derivative $\dot{\mathbf{x}}$-nu pakaram sample index $\mathbf{x}(k+1)$ varunnu. $s$-domain-le $(sI - A)$-nu pakaram $z$-transform-il $(zI - G)$ varunnu.

📌 ⚡ ഡിസ്ക്രീറ്റ് ട്രാൻസ്ഫർ ഫങ്ഷൻ
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$G(z) = C(zI - G)^{-1}H + D$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: $z$-ട്രാൻസ്ഫോർം ഉപയോഗിച്ച് ഡിജിറ്റൽ കൺട്രോൾ സിസ്റ്റങ്ങളിൽ വിശകലനം ചെയ്യുന്നു.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Discrete-Time State-Space Equations:
When a continuous system is sampled with period $T$, it is modeled by linear difference equations: $$\mathbf{x}(k+1) = G\mathbf{x}(k) + H\mathbf{u}(k) \quad \text{--- [State Equation]}$$ $$\mathbf{y}(k) = C\mathbf{x}(k) + D\mathbf{u}(k) \quad \text{--- [Output Equation]}$$ where $\mathbf{x}(k)$ is the state vector at sampling instant $t = kT$, and: $$G = e^{AT}, \quad H = \left(\int_0^T e^{At} dt\right) B$$

2. Pulse Transfer Function Derivation via Z-Transform:

  • Take the $z$-transform on both sides assuming zero initial states ($\mathbf{x}(0) = \mathbf{0}$): $$\mathcal{Z}\{\mathbf{x}(k+1)\} = z\mathbf{X}(z) = G\mathbf{X}(z) + H\mathbf{U}(z)$$ $$(zI - G)\mathbf{X}(z) = H\mathbf{U}(z) \implies \mathbf{X}(z) = (zI - G)^{-1} H \mathbf{U}(z)$$
  • Substitute $\mathbf{X}(z)$ into output equation $\mathbf{Y}(z) = C\mathbf{X}(z) + D\mathbf{U}(z)$: $$\mathbf{Y}(z) = \left[ C(zI - G)^{-1} H + D \right] \mathbf{U}(z)$$

Final Pulse Transfer Function: $$G(z) = \frac{\mathbf{Y}(z)}{\mathbf{U}(z)} = C(zI - G)^{-1} H + D$$

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

1. Continuous model-il sampling period $T$ apply cheythal: $G = e^{AT}$, $H = \int_0^T e^{A\tau} B d\tau$.

2. Taking $z$-transform with zero initial conditions: $z\mathbf{X}(z) - G\mathbf{X}(z) = H\mathbf{U}(z) \implies \mathbf{X}(z) = (zI - G)^{-1}H\mathbf{U}(z)$.

3. Output: $\mathbf{Y}(z) = [C(zI - G)^{-1}H + D]\mathbf{U}(z) \implies G(z) = \frac{\mathbf{Y}(z)}{\mathbf{U}(z)} = C(zI - G)^{-1}H + D$.

When a continuous system is sampled with period $T$, it is modeled by linear difference equations: $$\mathbf{x}(k+1) = G\mathbf{x}(k) + H\mathbf{u}(k) \quad \text{--- [State Equation]}$$ $$\mathbf{y}(k) = C\mathbf{x}(k) + D\mathbf{u}(k) \quad \text{--- [Output Equation]}$$ where $\mathbf{x}(k)$ is the state vector at sampling instant $t = kT$, and: $$G = e^{AT}, \quad H = \left(\int_0^T e^{At} dt\right) B$$

2. Pulse Transfer Function Derivation via Z-Transform:

  • Take the $z$-transform on both sides assuming zero initial states ($\mathbf{x}(0) = \mathbf{0}$): $$\mathcal{Z}\{\mathbf{x}(k+1)\} = z\mathbf{X}(z) = G\mathbf{X}(z) + H\mathbf{U}(z)$$ $$(zI - G)\mathbf{X}(z) = H\mathbf{U}(z) \implies \mathbf{X}(z) = (zI - G)^{-1} H \mathbf{U}(z)$$

സാമ്പ്ലിംഗ് ഇൻഡക്സ് $k$-ലും $z$-ഡൊമൈനിലും സമവാക്യങ്ങൾ രൂപീകരിക്കുക.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

1. Discrete-Time State-Space Equations:
When a continuous system is sampled with period $T$, it is modeled by linear difference equations: $$\mathbf{x}(k+1) = G\mathbf{x}(k) + H\mathbf{u}(k) \quad \text{--- [State Equation]}$$ $$\mathbf{y}(k) = C\mathbf{x}(k) + D\mathbf{u}(k) \quad \text{--- [Output Equation]}$$ where $\mathbf{x}(k)$ is the state vector at sampling instant $t = kT$, and: $$G = e^{AT}, \quad H = \left(\int_0^T e^{At} dt\right) B$$

2. Pulse Transfer Function Derivation via Z-Transform:

  • Take the $z$-transform on both sides assuming zero initial states ($\mathbf{x}(0) = \mathbf{0}$): $$\mathcal{Z}\{\mathbf{x}(k+1)\} = z\mathbf{X}(z) = G\mathbf{X}(z) + H\mathbf{U}(z)$$ $$(zI - G)\mathbf{X}(z) = H\mathbf{U}(z) \implies \mathbf{X}(z) = (zI - G)^{-1} H \mathbf{U}(z)$$
  • Substitute $\mathbf{X}(z)$ into output equation $\mathbf{Y}(z) = C\mathbf{X}(z) + D\mathbf{U}(z)$: $$\mathbf{Y}(z) = \left[ C(zI - G)^{-1} H + D \right] \mathbf{U}(z)$$

Final Pulse Transfer Function: $$G(z) = \frac{\mathbf{Y}(z)}{\mathbf{U}(z)} = C(zI - G)^{-1} H + D$$

Figure: Figure 2.2B: Discrete Delay Elements and State Transition in Sampled Data Systems

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

Continuous systems flow smoothly like an analog clock. But a microcontroller cannot watch continuous time—it takes snapshots every $T$ milliseconds (like a digital clock ticking). Discrete state space simply updates the state at tick $k+1$ using the state at tick $k$.

7/14 Marks (Long Essay / Derivation)
Long Derivation ID: ACS-Q10

Explain the computation of state transition matrix e^{At} using Cayley-Hamilton theorem for both distinct and repeated eigenvalues. Cayley-Hamilton Theorem use cheythu State Transition Matrix $e^{At}$ engane kandupidikkam? കെയ്‌ലി-ഹാമിൽട്ടൺ തിയറം ഉപയോഗിച്ച് സ്റ്റേറ്റ് ട്രാൻസിഷൻ മാട്രിക്സ് എങ്ങനെ കണക്കാക്കാം?

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$e^{At} = \sum_{k=0}^{n-1} \alpha_k(t) A^k, \quad e^{\lambda_i t} = \sum_{k=0}^{n-1} \alpha_k(t) \lambda_i^k$$

Every square matrix satisfies its characteristic polynomial; reduces infinite matrix series $e^{At}$ to a degree $(n-1)$ polynomial.

📌 ⚡ Cayley-Hamilton Matrix Polynomial
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\Delta(A) = A^n + a_{n-1}A^{n-1} + \dots + a_0 I = \mathbf{0}, \quad e^{At} = \alpha_0(t)I + \alpha_1(t)A + \dots + \alpha_{n-1}(t)A^{n-1}$$

Ormavekkan: Statement: 'Every square matrix satisfies its own characteristic equation'. Infinite series $e^{At}$-ne finite $(n-1)$-th order polynomial aakki maattam!

📌 ⚡ തിയറം പ്രസ്താവന
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$e^{At} = \alpha_0 I + \alpha_1 A + \dots + \alpha_{n-1} A^{n-1}$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: 'ഓരോ സ്ക്വയർ മാട്രിക്സും സ്വന്തം സ്വഭാവ സമവാക്യം അനുസരിക്കുന്നു.' അനന്തമായ പദങ്ങളെ പരിമിതമായ മാട്രിക്സ് രൂപത്തിലാക്കാം.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Statement of Cayley-Hamilton Theorem:
Every square matrix $A$ of order $n \times n$ satisfies its own characteristic equation: $$\Delta(\lambda) = \det(\lambda I - A) = \lambda^n + a_{n-1}\lambda^{n-1} + \dots + a_1\lambda + a_0 = 0$$ $$\implies A^n + a_{n-1}A^{n-1} + \dots + a_1 A + a_0 I = \mathbf{0}$$

2. Theoretical Principle:
The matrix exponential is an infinite series: $e^{At} = I + At + \frac{A^2 t^2}{2!} + \dots$
Using Cayley-Hamilton, any higher power $A^k$ ($k \ge n$) can be expressed as a linear combination of lower powers $I, A, A^2, \dots, A^{n-1}$.
Therefore, $e^{At}$ reduces to a finite polynomial of degree $(n-1)$: $$e^{At} = \alpha_0(t)I + \alpha_1(t)A + \alpha_2(t)A^2 + \dots + \alpha_{n-1}(t)A^{n-1} \quad \text{--- (1)}$$ where $\alpha_0(t), \alpha_1(t), \dots, \alpha_{n-1}(t)$ are scalar functions of time.

3. Step-by-Step Procedure:

  1. Find Eigenvalues: Solve characteristic equation $\det(\lambda I - A) = 0$ to get $n$ eigenvalues $\lambda_1, \lambda_2, \dots, \lambda_n$.
  2. Form Scalar Equation: The scalar function $e^{\lambda t}$ satisfies the same polynomial: $$e^{\lambda_i t} = \alpha_0(t) + \alpha_1(t)\lambda_i + \alpha_2(t)\lambda_i^2 + \dots + \alpha_{n-1}(t)\lambda_i^{n-1}$$
  3. Case I (Distinct Eigenvalues): Substitute each distinct $\lambda_i$ into the scalar equation. This gives $n$ equations in $n$ unknowns ($\alpha_0, \dots, \alpha_{n-1}$). Solve this linear algebraic system.
  4. Case II (Repeated Eigenvalues): If eigenvalue $\lambda_1$ is repeated with multiplicity $m$, differentiate with respect to $\lambda$: $$\frac{d^k}{d\lambda^k}\left[e^{\lambda t}\right]_{\lambda = \lambda_1} = \frac{d^k}{d\lambda^k}\left[\sum_{j=0}^{n-1} \alpha_j(t)\lambda^j\right]_{\lambda = \lambda_1}, \quad k = 1, 2, \dots, m-1$$
  5. Substitute back into Matrix Formula: Put computed scalar functions $\alpha_k(t)$ into Equation (1) to get the final matrix $e^{At}$.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

Step-by-Step Procedure:

  1. Find characteristic equation: $\det(\lambda I - A) = 0 \implies \lambda_1, \lambda_2, \dots, \lambda_n$.
  2. Set scalar equation for each eigenvalue: $e^{\lambda_i t} = \alpha_0(t) + \alpha_1(t)\lambda_i + \dots + \alpha_{n-1}(t)\lambda_i^{n-1}$.
  3. Solve for unknown scalar functions $\alpha_0(t), \alpha_1(t), \dots$.
  4. Substitute scalars into matrix equation: $e^{At} = \alpha_0(t)I + \alpha_1(t)A + \dots + \alpha_{n-1}(t)A^{n-1}$.

Every square matrix $A$ of order $n \times n$ satisfies its own characteristic equation: $$\Delta(\lambda) = \det(\lambda I - A) = \lambda^n + a_{n-1}\lambda^{n-1} + \dots + a_1\lambda + a_0 = 0$$ $$\implies A^n + a_{n-1}A^{n-1} + \dots + a_1 A + a_0 I = \mathbf{0}$$

ഐഗൻവാല്യൂസ് കണ്ടെത്തി $\alpha_i(t)$ കണ്ടുപിടിച്ച് $e^{At}$ മാട്രിക്സ് പോളിനോമിയലിൽ സബ്സ്റ്റിറ്റ്യൂട്ട് ചെയ്യുക.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

1. Statement of Cayley-Hamilton Theorem:

3. Step-by-Step Procedure:

  1. Find Eigenvalues: Solve characteristic equation $\det(\lambda I - A) = 0$ to get $n$ eigenvalues $\lambda_1, \lambda_2, \dots, \lambda_n$.
  2. Form Scalar Equation: The scalar function $e^{\lambda t}$ satisfies the same polynomial: $$e^{\lambda_i t} = \alpha_0(t) + \alpha_1(t)\lambda_i + \alpha_2(t)\lambda_i^2 + \dots + \alpha_{n-1}(t)\lambda_i^{n-1}$$
  3. Case I (Distinct Eigenvalues): Substitute each distinct $\lambda_i$ into the scalar equation. This gives $n$ equations in $n$ unknowns ($\alpha_0, \dots, \alpha_{n-1}$). Solve this linear algebraic system.
  4. Case II (Repeated Eigenvalues): If eigenvalue $\lambda_1$ is repeated with multiplicity $m$, differentiate with respect to $\lambda$: $$\frac{d^k}{d\lambda^k}\left[e^{\lambda t}\right]_{\lambda = \lambda_1} = \frac{d^k}{d\lambda^k}\left[\sum_{j=0}^{n-1} \alpha_j(t)\lambda^j\right]_{\lambda = \lambda_1}, \quad k = 1, 2, \dots, m-1$$
  5. Substitute back into Matrix Formula: Put computed scalar functions $\alpha_k(t)$ into Equation (1) to get the final matrix $e^{At}$.

Figure: Figure 2.3: Mapping Characteristic Polynomial to Matrix Exponential via Cayley-Hamilton

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

Calculating $e^{At}$ directly means multiplying matrices an infinite number of times—impossible! The Cayley-Hamilton theorem gives a massive shortcut: for a $2 \times 2$ matrix, $A^2$ can always be rewritten in terms of $A$ and $I$. So the infinite series collapses into just two terms: $\alpha_0 I + \alpha_1 A$. You only have to find two numbers $\alpha_0$ and $\alpha_1$ using simple high-school algebra on the eigenvalues!

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

$e^{At} = I + At + \frac{A^2t^2}{2!} + \dots$ ennath infinite terms ulla oru valiya list aanu. Pakshe Cayley-Hamilton theorem oru shortcut tharunnu: $A^n$-ne lower powers $A^{n-1}, \dots, I$ aakki substitute cheythu finite terms aakki mathram calculate cheythaal mathi!

💡 ലളിതമായി മനസ്സിലാക്കാം — പ്രാദേശിക ഉദാഹരണം
🇮🇳 മലയാളം ▼

അനന്തമായി നീളുന്ന സീരീസിനെ ചുരുക്കി എളുപ്പത്തിൽ കണക്കുകൂട്ടാനുള്ള കുറുക്കുവഴിയാണിത്!

7/14 Marks (Long Essay / Derivation)
Long Derivation ID: ACS-Q11

Derive the complete solution of non-homogeneous continuous-time state equations and determine the step response expression. Non-Homogeneous State Equation $\dot{\mathbf{x}} = A\mathbf{x} + B\mathbf{u}$-nte complete solution engane derive cheyyam? നോൺ-ഹോമോജീനിയസ് സ്റ്റേറ്റ് സമവാക്യത്തിന്റെ പൂർണ്ണ പരിഹാരം എങ്ങനെ കണ്ടെത്താം?

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$\mathbf{x}(t) = e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0) + \int_0^t e^{A(t-\tau)}B\mathbf{u}(\tau)d\tau$$

Total Response = Zero-Input Response (ZIR) + Zero-State Response (ZSR via convolution integral).

📌 ⚡ Complete Response = ZIR + ZSR
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\mathbf{x}(t) = \underbrace{e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0)}_{\text{Zero-Input Response (ZIR)}} + \underbrace{\int_0^t e^{A(t - \tau)} B \mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau}_{\text{Zero-State Response (ZSR)}}$$

Ormavekkan: Total response = Initial condition response (ZIR) + External input convolution integral response (ZSR). Integrating factor $e^{-At}$ premultiply cheythu direct aayi integrate cheyyam.

📌 ⚡ പൂർണ്ണ പ്രതികരണം
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\mathbf{x}(t) = e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0) + \int_0^t e^{A(t - \tau)} B \mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: ആകെ പ്രതികരണം = പ്രാരംഭ അവസ്ഥയുടെ പ്രതികരണം (ZIR) + ഇൻപുട്ട് കൊണ്ടുണ്ടാകുന്ന പ്രതികരണം (ZSR).

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Problem Statement:
Consider the complete continuous-time state equation with non-zero input $\mathbf{u}(t)$ and initial condition $\mathbf{x}(0)$: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = A\mathbf{x}(t) + B\mathbf{u}(t), \quad t \ge 0$$

2. Derivation using Integrating Factor:

  • Rearrange the state equation: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) - A\mathbf{x}(t) = B\mathbf{u}(t)$$
  • Premultiply both sides by the integrating factor $e^{-At}$: $$e^{-At}\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) - e^{-At}A\mathbf{x}(t) = e^{-At}B\mathbf{u}(t)$$
  • Notice that the left-hand side is the exact derivative of a product: $$\frac{d}{dt}\left[ e^{-At}\mathbf{x}(t) \right] = e^{-At}B\mathbf{u}(t)$$
  • Integrate both sides from $\tau = 0$ to $\tau = t$: $$\int_0^t \frac{d}{d\tau}\left[ e^{-A\tau}\mathbf{x}(\tau) \right] d\tau = \int_0^t e^{-A\tau}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau$$ $$e^{-At}\mathbf{x}(t) - e^{-A(0)}\mathbf{x}(0) = \int_0^t e^{-A\tau}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau$$ Since $e^0 = I$: $$e^{-At}\mathbf{x}(t) = \mathbf{x}(0) + \int_0^t e^{-A\tau}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau$$
  • Premultiply all terms by $e^{At}$: $$\mathbf{x}(t) = e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0) + e^{At} \int_0^t e^{-A\tau}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau$$ Moving $e^{At}$ inside the integral (since integration is with respect to $\tau$): $$\mathbf{x}(t) = e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0) + \int_0^t e^{A(t-\tau)}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau$$

3. Structure of the Complete Solution:

$$\mathbf{x}(t) = \underbrace{e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0)}_{\text{Zero-Input Response (ZIR)}} + \underbrace{\int_0^t e^{A(t-\tau)}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau}_{\text{Zero-State Response (ZSR)}}$$

Output Response: $$\mathbf{y}(t) = C\mathbf{x}(t) + D\mathbf{u}(t) = C e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0) + C \int_0^t e^{A(t-\tau)}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau + D\mathbf{u}(t)$$

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

1. Rewrite: $\dot{\mathbf{x}} - A\mathbf{x} = B\mathbf{u}$.

2. Premultiply by integrating factor $e^{-At}$: $e^{-At}\dot{\mathbf{x}} - e^{-At}A\mathbf{x} = \frac{d}{dt}[e^{-At}\mathbf{x}(t)] = e^{-At}B\mathbf{u}(t)$.

3. Integrate from $0$ to $t$: $e^{-At}\mathbf{x}(t) - \mathbf{x}(0) = \int_0^t e^{-A\tau}B\mathbf{u}(\tau)d\tau$.

4. Premultiply by $e^{At}$: $\mathbf{x}(t) = e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0) + \int_0^t e^{A(t - \tau)}B\mathbf{u}(\tau)d\tau$.

📝 Exact English Text for University Examination:

1. Problem Statement:
Consider the complete continuous-time state equation with non-zero input $\mathbf{u}(t)$ and initial condition $\mathbf{x}(0)$: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = A\mathbf{x}(t) + B\mathbf{u}(t), \quad t \ge 0$$

2. Derivation using Integrating Factor:

  • Rearrange the state equation: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) - A\mathbf{x}(t) = B\mathbf{u}(t)$$
  • Premultiply both sides by the integrating factor $e^{-At}$: $$e^{-At}\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) - e^{-At}A\mathbf{x}(t) = e^{-At}B\mathbf{u}(t)$$
  • Notice that the left-hand side is the exact derivative of a product: $$\frac{d}{dt}\left[ e^{-At}\mathbf{x}(t) \right] = e^{-At}B\mathbf{u}(t)$$
  • Integrate both sides from $\tau = 0$ to $\tau = t$: $$\int_0^t \frac{d}{d\tau}\left[ e^{-A\tau}\mathbf{x}(\tau) \right] d\tau = \int_0^t e^{-A\tau}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau$$ $$e^{-At}\mathbf{x}(t) - e^{-A(0)}\mathbf{x}(0) = \int_0^t e^{-A\tau}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau$$ Since $e^0 = I$: $$e^{-At}\mathbf{x}(t) = \mathbf{x}(0) + \int_0^t e^{-A\tau}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau$$
  • Premultiply all terms by $e^{At}$: $$\mathbf{x}(t) = e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0) + e^{At} \int_0^t e^{-A\tau}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau$$ Moving $e^{At}$ inside the integral (since integration is with respect to $\tau$): $$\mathbf{x}(t) = e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0) + \int_0^t e^{A(t-\tau)}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau$$

3. Structure of the Complete Solution:

$$\mathbf{x}(t) = \underbrace{e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0)}_{\text{Zero-Input Response (ZIR)}} + \underbrace{\int_0^t e^{A(t-\tau)}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau}_{\text{Zero-State Response (ZSR)}}$$

Output Response: $$\mathbf{y}(t) = C\mathbf{x}(t) + D\mathbf{u}(t) = C e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0) + C \int_0^t e^{A(t-\tau)}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau + D\mathbf{u}(t)$$

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പഠന സഹായി

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

1. Problem Statement:
Consider the complete continuous-time state equation with non-zero input $\mathbf{u}(t)$ and initial condition $\mathbf{x}(0)$: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) = A\mathbf{x}(t) + B\mathbf{u}(t), \quad t \ge 0$$

2. Derivation using Integrating Factor:

  • Rearrange the state equation: $$\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) - A\mathbf{x}(t) = B\mathbf{u}(t)$$
  • Premultiply both sides by the integrating factor $e^{-At}$: $$e^{-At}\dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) - e^{-At}A\mathbf{x}(t) = e^{-At}B\mathbf{u}(t)$$
  • Notice that the left-hand side is the exact derivative of a product: $$\frac{d}{dt}\left[ e^{-At}\mathbf{x}(t) \right] = e^{-At}B\mathbf{u}(t)$$
  • Integrate both sides from $\tau = 0$ to $\tau = t$: $$\int_0^t \frac{d}{d\tau}\left[ e^{-A\tau}\mathbf{x}(\tau) \right] d\tau = \int_0^t e^{-A\tau}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau$$ $$e^{-At}\mathbf{x}(t) - e^{-A(0)}\mathbf{x}(0) = \int_0^t e^{-A\tau}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau$$ Since $e^0 = I$: $$e^{-At}\mathbf{x}(t) = \mathbf{x}(0) + \int_0^t e^{-A\tau}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau$$
  • Premultiply all terms by $e^{At}$: $$\mathbf{x}(t) = e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0) + e^{At} \int_0^t e^{-A\tau}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau$$ Moving $e^{At}$ inside the integral (since integration is with respect to $\tau$): $$\mathbf{x}(t) = e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0) + \int_0^t e^{A(t-\tau)}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau$$

3. Structure of the Complete Solution:

$$\mathbf{x}(t) = \underbrace{e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0)}_{\text{Zero-Input Response (ZIR)}} + \underbrace{\int_0^t e^{A(t-\tau)}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau}_{\text{Zero-State Response (ZSR)}}$$

Output Response: $$\mathbf{y}(t) = C\mathbf{x}(t) + D\mathbf{u}(t) = C e^{At}\mathbf{x}(0) + C \int_0^t e^{A(t-\tau)}B\mathbf{u}(\tau) d\tau + D\mathbf{u}(t)$$

Figure: Figure 2.4: Superposition of Zero-Input and Zero-State Convolution Integral Responses

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

When you drive a car, its total movement comes from two things: (1) where the car was already rolling due to its past momentum (Zero-Input Response), plus (2) how hard you are pressing the gas pedal right now (Zero-State Response). The convolution integral simply sums up the effect of every tap of your foot from time $0$ to time $t$.

Oru car-inte total speed nokkiyaal: 1) Initial speed karanam rolling cheyyunna part (ZIR), plus 2) Driver accelerator pedal chavitti kodukkunna power karanam koodunna speed (ZSR). Ee randum koodi add cheythaal total speed kitum!

Module 3

Controllability, Observability & State Feedback Controller / Observer Design

5 Elaborated University Questions & Answers

3 Marks (Short Answer)
Short Answer ID: ACS-Q12

State and explain Kalman's duality principle relating state controllability and state observability. Kalman's Duality Principle between Controllability and Observability explain cheyyuka. കാൽമാന്റെ ഡ്യുവാലിറ്റി തത്വം (Duality Principle) വിശദീകരിക്കുക

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$\text{Controllability of } (A, B) \iff \text{Observability of } (A^T, C^T)$$

Controllability and observability are mathematical duals; transpose matrices to switch between them.

📌 ⚡ Duality Principle Mapping
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$[A, B] \text{ is Controllable} \iff [A^T, B^T] \text{ is Observable}, \quad Q_o(A^T, B^T) = Q_c^T(A, B)$$

Ormavekkan: Controllability problem-ine transpose matrix $A^T, C^T$ vechu direct aayi observability problem aakki maattam. Control controller design-um observer design-um duals aanu.

📌 ⚡ ഡ്യുവാലിറ്റി തത്വം
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$[A, B] \text{ controllable} \iff [A^T, B^T] \text{ observable}$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: കൺട്രോളബിലിറ്റിയും ഒബ്സർവബിലിറ്റിയും ഗണിതശാസ്ത്രപരമായി ഒന്നിന്റെ ട്രാൻസ്പോസ് മറ്റൊന്നായ ഡ്യുവൽ പ്രോപ്പർട്ടികളാണ്.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Statement of Kalman's Duality Principle:

  • Kalman's duality principle states that a system $S_1$ described by the pair $(A, B)$ is completely state controllable if and only if its dual system $S_2$ described by the pair $(A^T, B^T)$ is completely state observable.
  • Conversely, a system $(A, C)$ is completely state observable if and only if its dual system $(A^T, C^T)$ is completely state controllable.

2. Mathematical Proof using Rank Conditions:

  • The Controllability Matrix for $(A, B)$ is: $$Q_c = \begin{bmatrix} B & AB & A^2B & \dots & A^{n-1}B \end{bmatrix}$$
  • The Observability Matrix for $(A, C)$ is: $$Q_0 = \begin{bmatrix} C \\ CA \\ CA^2 \\ \vdots \\ CA^{n-1} \end{bmatrix}$$
  • Taking the transpose of $Q_0$: $$Q_0^T = \begin{bmatrix} C^T & A^T C^T & (A^T)^2 C^T & \dots & (A^T)^{n-1} C^T \end{bmatrix}$$
  • Notice that $Q_0^T$ has the exact same mathematical form as the controllability matrix of the dual pair $(A^T, C^T)$. Since the rank of any matrix equals the rank of its transpose ($\operatorname{rank}(Q_0) = \operatorname{rank}(Q_0^T)$): $$\operatorname{rank}(Q_c) = n \iff \operatorname{rank}(Q_0) = n$$

Practical Engineering Use: Controller design algorithms (like pole placement feedback $K$) can be directly converted into state observer design algorithms (observer gain $L$) by replacing $A \to A^T$ and $B \to C^T$.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

1. System $S_1 = (A, B, C)$. Controllability matrix $Q_c = [B \;\; AB \;\; \dots \;\; A^{n-1}B]$.

2. Dual system $S_2 = (A^T, C^T, B^T)$. Observability matrix $Q_o = [C^T \;\; A^T C^T \;\; \dots]^T$.

3. Notice that $Q_o(A^T, B^T) = Q_c^T(A, B)$. So $\operatorname{rank}(Q_c) = n \iff \operatorname{rank}(Q_o) = n$.

Significance: Ackermann's pole placement code transpose cheythaal Luenberger observer design direct aayi cheyyam!

📝 Exact English Text for University Examination:

1. Statement of Kalman's Duality Principle:

  • Kalman's duality principle states that a system $S_1$ described by the pair $(A, B)$ is completely state controllable if and only if its dual system $S_2$ described by the pair $(A^T, B^T)$ is completely state observable.
  • Conversely, a system $(A, C)$ is completely state observable if and only if its dual system $(A^T, C^T)$ is completely state controllable.

2. Mathematical Proof using Rank Conditions:

  • The Controllability Matrix for $(A, B)$ is: $$Q_c = \begin{bmatrix} B & AB & A^2B & \dots & A^{n-1}B \end{bmatrix}$$
  • The Observability Matrix for $(A, C)$ is: $$Q_0 = \begin{bmatrix} C \\ CA \\ CA^2 \\ \vdots \\ CA^{n-1} \end{bmatrix}$$
  • Taking the transpose of $Q_0$: $$Q_0^T = \begin{bmatrix} C^T & A^T C^T & (A^T)^2 C^T & \dots & (A^T)^{n-1} C^T \end{bmatrix}$$
  • Notice that $Q_0^T$ has the exact same mathematical form as the controllability matrix of the dual pair $(A^T, C^T)$. Since the rank of any matrix equals the rank of its transpose ($\operatorname{rank}(Q_0) = \operatorname{rank}(Q_0^T)$): $$\operatorname{rank}(Q_c) = n \iff \operatorname{rank}(Q_0) = n$$

Practical Engineering Use: Controller design algorithms (like pole placement feedback $K$) can be directly converted into state observer design algorithms (observer gain $L$) by replacing $A \to A^T$ and $B \to C^T$.

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പ്രാദേശിക പഠന സഹായി

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പഠന സഹായി

മാട്രിക്സ് ട്രാൻസ്പോസ് വഴി $Q_o = Q_c^T$ എന്ന് കാണിച്ച് തുല്യത തെളിയിക്കുക.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

1. Statement of Kalman's Duality Principle:

  • Kalman's duality principle states that a system $S_1$ described by the pair $(A, B)$ is completely state controllable if and only if its dual system $S_2$ described by the pair $(A^T, B^T)$ is completely state observable.
  • Conversely, a system $(A, C)$ is completely state observable if and only if its dual system $(A^T, C^T)$ is completely state controllable.

2. Mathematical Proof using Rank Conditions:

  • The Controllability Matrix for $(A, B)$ is: $$Q_c = \begin{bmatrix} B & AB & A^2B & \dots & A^{n-1}B \end{bmatrix}$$
  • The Observability Matrix for $(A, C)$ is: $$Q_0 = \begin{bmatrix} C \\ CA \\ CA^2 \\ \vdots \\ CA^{n-1} \end{bmatrix}$$
  • Taking the transpose of $Q_0$: $$Q_0^T = \begin{bmatrix} C^T & A^T C^T & (A^T)^2 C^T & \dots & (A^T)^{n-1} C^T \end{bmatrix}$$
  • Notice that $Q_0^T$ has the exact same mathematical form as the controllability matrix of the dual pair $(A^T, C^T)$. Since the rank of any matrix equals the rank of its transpose ($\operatorname{rank}(Q_0) = \operatorname{rank}(Q_0^T)$): $$\operatorname{rank}(Q_c) = n \iff \operatorname{rank}(Q_0) = n$$

Practical Engineering Use: Controller design algorithms (like pole placement feedback $K$) can be directly converted into state observer design algorithms (observer gain $L$) by replacing $A \to A^T$ and $B \to C^T$.

Figure: Figure 3.1: Dual Mapping between Controllability and Observability Matrices

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

Controllability means: "Can my steering wheel reach and move every part of the car?" Observability means: "Can my dashboard gauges see and measure every part of the car?" Kalman proved that mathematically, steering a system forward is the exact mirror-image (transpose) of watching a system backward!

🌴 Local Tone ▼

Oru mirror reflection pole aanu duality: Mirror-te oru vashathu controller design irunnal, thirichulla reflection aanu observer design. Mathematics same aanu, matrics transpose cheythaal mathi!

3 Marks (Short Answer)
Short Answer ID: ACS-Q13

Explain Gilbert's method for determining the state controllability and state observability of an LTI continuous system. Gilbert's Method use cheythu Controllability-um Observability-um engane test cheyyam? ഗിൽബർട്ട്സ് രീതി (Gilbert's Method) ഉപയോഗിച്ച് കൺട്രോളബിലിറ്റിയും ഒബ്സർവബിലിറ്റിയും എങ്ങനെ പരിശോധിക്കാം?

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$\dot{\mathbf{z}} = \Lambda \mathbf{z} + \bar{B}\mathbf{u}, \quad \mathbf{y} = \bar{C}\mathbf{z}$$

In diagonalized form: Controllable if $\bar{B}$ has no zero rows; Observable if $\bar{C}$ has no zero columns.

📌 ⚡ Gilbert's Non-Zero Row/Column Test
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\dot{\mathbf{z}} = \Lambda \mathbf{z} + \bar{B}\mathbf{u}, \quad \mathbf{y} = \bar{C}\mathbf{z}, \quad \bar{B} = M^{-1}B, \quad \bar{C} = CM$$

Ormavekkan: Distinct eigenvalues aayirikkumbol: $\bar{B}$-il oru row full zero aaneel uncontrollable! $\bar{C}$-il oru column full zero aaneel unobservable!

📌 ⚡ ഗിൽബർട്ട്സ് നിബന്ധന
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\bar{B} \text{ row zero } \implies \text{Uncontrollable}, \quad \bar{C} \text{ col zero } \implies \text{Unobservable}$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: ഡയഗണൽ രൂപത്തിൽ $\bar{B}$-യിലെ ഒരു വരി മുഴുവൻ പൂജ്യമായാൽ uncontrollable; $\bar{C}$-യിലെ കോളം പൂജ്യമായാൽ unobservable.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Gilbert's Method Principle:
Gilbert's method tests controllability and observability by converting the system into a diagonal canonical form using an eigenvector modal matrix $M$.

2. Condition for Distinct Eigenvalues:

  1. Find the eigenvalues $\lambda_1, \lambda_2, \dots, \lambda_n$ of matrix $A$. Assume all eigenvalues are distinct.
  2. Construct the modal matrix $M = \begin{bmatrix} \mathbf{v}_1 & \mathbf{v}_2 & \dots & \mathbf{v}_n \end{bmatrix}$ whose columns are the eigenvectors of $A$.
  3. Transform the system into diagonal form $\mathbf{x} = M\mathbf{z}$: $$\Lambda = M^{-1}AM = \operatorname{diag}(\lambda_1, \lambda_2, \dots, \lambda_n)$$ $$\bar{B} = M^{-1}B, \quad \bar{C} = CM$$
  4. Controllability Test: The system is completely state controllable if and only if the transformed input matrix $\bar{B}$ has no row containing all zeros. (If row $i$ of $\bar{B}$ is zero, mode $\lambda_i$ cannot be affected by any input).
  5. Observability Test: The system is completely state observable if and only if the transformed output matrix $\bar{C}$ has no column containing all zeros. (If column $j$ of $\bar{C}$ is zero, mode $\lambda_j$ has zero contribution to the output).

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

Steps:

  1. Diagonalize system: $\Lambda = M^{-1}AM = \operatorname{diag}(\lambda_1, \dots, \lambda_n)$.
  2. Compute transformed input matrix $\bar{B} = M^{-1}B$. Check each row: if row $i$ has all zeros, mode $\lambda_i$ cannot be controlled!
  3. Compute transformed output matrix $\bar{C} = CM$. Check each column: if column $j$ has all zeros, mode $\lambda_j$ cannot be observed!

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പ്രാദേശിക പഠന സഹായി

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പഠന സഹായി

ഡയഗണലൈസ് ചെയ്ത് $\bar{B}, \bar{C}$ മാട്രിക്സുകളിലെ പൂജ്യങ്ങൾ പരിശോധിക്കുക.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

  5. Observability Test: The system is completely state observable if and only if the transformed output matrix $\bar{C}$ has no column containing all zeros. (If column $j$ of $\bar{C}$ is zero, mode $\lambda_j$ has zero contribution to the output).

Figure: Figure 3.2: Modal Decoupling and Gilbert's Diagonal Row/Column Inspection

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

Imagine 3 independent electric motors. Gilbert's method simply asks two common-sense questions: (1) Does every motor have a power wire connected to the throttle ($\bar{B}$ has no empty rows)? If yes, you can control all of them. (2) Does every motor have a speed sensor reporting to the screen ($\bar{C}$ has no empty columns)? If yes, you can observe all of them.

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

Oru switchboard nokku: Oro bulb-num oro switch undu (Diagonalized modes). Oru bulb-ilottu wire connect cheythittillel ($\bar{B}$ row zero), switch ittaalum bulb kathilla (uncontrollable). Oru bulb kathunnathu puratheku kaanan glass window illel ($\bar{C}$ column zero), athu unobservable aanu!

3 Marks (Short Answer)
Short Answer ID: ACS-Q13B

State and explain the Popov-Belevitch-Hautus (PBH) rank tests for testing controllability and observability. Popov-Belevitch-Hautus (PBH) Rank Tests for Controllability and Observability explain cheyyuka. PBH (Popov-Belevitch-Hautus) റാങ്ക് ടെസ്റ്റുകൾ വിശദീകരിക്കുക

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$\operatorname{rank}[sI - A \quad B] = n, \quad \operatorname{rank}\begin{bmatrix} sI - A \\ C \end{bmatrix} = n \quad \forall s = \lambda_i$$

PBH test checks matrix rank only at the eigenvalues $\lambda_i$ of matrix $A$.

📌 ⚡ PBH Rank Conditions
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\operatorname{rank}[sI - A \quad B] = n, \quad \operatorname{rank}\begin{bmatrix} sI - A \\ C \end{bmatrix} = n \quad \forall s = \lambda_i$$

Ormavekkan: Matrix $A$-yude oro eigenvalue $\lambda_i$ eduthu substitute cheythu check cheythal mathi. Kalman test pole high powers $A^{n-1}$ calculate cheyyenda aavashyame illa!

📌 ⚡ PBH റാങ്ക് നിബന്ധന
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\operatorname{rank}[\lambda_i I - A \quad B] = n, \quad \operatorname{rank}\begin{bmatrix} \lambda_i I - A \\ C \end{bmatrix} = n$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: എല്ലാ ഐഗൻവാല്യൂസിനും മാട്രിക്സ് റാങ്ക് $n$ ആണെങ്കിൽ സിസ്റ്റം കൺട്രോളബിളും ഒബ്സർവബിളും ആയിരിക്കും.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. PBH Controllability Test:

  • The continuous-time LTI system $(A, B)$ is completely state controllable if and only if: $$\operatorname{rank}\begin{bmatrix} sI - A & B \end{bmatrix} = n \quad \text{for all complex } s \in \mathbb{C}$$
  • Since $(sI - A)$ has full rank $n$ for any $s$ that is not an eigenvalue, we only need to test this rank condition at the eigenvalues $\lambda_1, \dots, \lambda_n$ of matrix $A$: $$\operatorname{rank}\begin{bmatrix} \lambda_i I - A & B \end{bmatrix} = n, \quad \forall i = 1, 2, \dots, n$$

2. PBH Observability Test:

  • The continuous-time LTI system $(A, C)$ is completely state observable if and only if: $$\operatorname{rank}\begin{bmatrix} sI - A \\ C \end{bmatrix} = n \quad \text{for all complex } s \in \mathbb{C}$$
  • Equivalently, evaluated only at each eigenvalue $\lambda_i$: $$\operatorname{rank}\begin{bmatrix} \lambda_i I - A \\ C \end{bmatrix} = n, \quad \forall i = 1, 2, \dots, n$$

Major Advantage over Kalman Test: When an eigenvalue fails the rank condition, PBH identifies the exact uncontrollable or unobservable dynamic mode without computing matrix powers $A^2, A^3, \dots, A^{n-1}$.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

1. PBH Controllability: System controllable aakanamenkil, matrix $M_c(s) = [sI - A \;\; B]$ has rank $n$ for all eigenvalues $s = \lambda_1, \dots, \lambda_n$.

2. PBH Observability: System observable aakanamenkil, matrix $M_o(s) = \begin{bmatrix} sI - A \\ C \end{bmatrix}$ has rank $n$ for all eigenvalues $s = \lambda_i$.

Advantage: Repeated eigenvalues varumbolum PBH test accurate aayi work cheyyum!

📝 Exact English Text for University Examination:

1. PBH Controllability Test:

  • The continuous-time LTI system $(A, B)$ is completely state controllable if and only if: $$\operatorname{rank}\begin{bmatrix} sI - A & B \end{bmatrix} = n \quad \text{for all complex } s \in \mathbb{C}$$
  • Since $(sI - A)$ has full rank $n$ for any $s$ that is not an eigenvalue, we only need to test this rank condition at the eigenvalues $\lambda_1, \dots, \lambda_n$ of matrix $A$: $$\operatorname{rank}\begin{bmatrix} \lambda_i I - A & B \end{bmatrix} = n, \quad \forall i = 1, 2, \dots, n$$

2. PBH Observability Test:

  • The continuous-time LTI system $(A, C)$ is completely state observable if and only if: $$\operatorname{rank}\begin{bmatrix} sI - A \\ C \end{bmatrix} = n \quad \text{for all complex } s \in \mathbb{C}$$
  • Equivalently, evaluated only at each eigenvalue $\lambda_i$: $$\operatorname{rank}\begin{bmatrix} \lambda_i I - A \\ C \end{bmatrix} = n, \quad \forall i = 1, 2, \dots, n$$

Major Advantage over Kalman Test: When an eigenvalue fails the rank condition, PBH identifies the exact uncontrollable or unobservable dynamic mode without computing matrix powers $A^2, A^3, \dots, A^{n-1}$.

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പ്രാദേശിക പഠന സഹായി

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പഠന സഹായി

ഓരോ ഐഗൻവാല്യൂവിലും മാട്രിക്സ് റാങ്ക് $n$ ആണെന്ന് കാണിച്ച് ടെസ്റ്റ് വിശദീകരിക്കുക.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

1. PBH Controllability Test:

  • The continuous-time LTI system $(A, B)$ is completely state controllable if and only if: $$\operatorname{rank}\begin{bmatrix} sI - A & B \end{bmatrix} = n \quad \text{for all complex } s \in \mathbb{C}$$
  • Since $(sI - A)$ has full rank $n$ for any $s$ that is not an eigenvalue, we only need to test this rank condition at the eigenvalues $\lambda_1, \dots, \lambda_n$ of matrix $A$: $$\operatorname{rank}\begin{bmatrix} \lambda_i I - A & B \end{bmatrix} = n, \quad \forall i = 1, 2, \dots, n$$

2. PBH Observability Test:

  • The continuous-time LTI system $(A, C)$ is completely state observable if and only if: $$\operatorname{rank}\begin{bmatrix} sI - A \\ C \end{bmatrix} = n \quad \text{for all complex } s \in \mathbb{C}$$
  • Equivalently, evaluated only at each eigenvalue $\lambda_i$: $$\operatorname{rank}\begin{bmatrix} \lambda_i I - A \\ C \end{bmatrix} = n, \quad \forall i = 1, 2, \dots, n$$

Major Advantage over Kalman Test: When an eigenvalue fails the rank condition, PBH identifies the exact uncontrollable or unobservable dynamic mode without computing matrix powers $A^2, A^3, \dots, A^{n-1}$.

Figure: Figure 3.2B: Modal Partitioning in Popov-Belevitch-Hautus (PBH) Algebraic Rank Test

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

Kalman's test computes massive matrix multiplications ($A^2, A^3, \dots$) which can cause big numerical rounding errors. The PBH test is like a doctor testing specific pressure points: you plug in only the natural frequencies (eigenvalues $\lambda_i$) of the system and check if the matrix stays healthy (full rank).

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

Kalman test oru valiya powers calculating procedure aanu ($A, A^2, A^3 \dots$). Pakshe PBH test direct aayi eigenvalues substitute cheythu matrix rank nokkunna valare smart and fast method aanu!

🇮🇳 മലയാളം ▼

വലിയ ഗുണനങ്ങളില്ലാതെ നേരിട്ട് ഐഗൻവാല്യൂസ് വെച്ച് പരിശോധിക്കാനുള്ള എളുപ്പവഴിയാണിത്!

7/14 Marks (Long Essay / Derivation)
Long Derivation ID: ACS-Q14

Explain the design of a state feedback controller by pole placement technique and derive Ackermann's formula. Pole Placement State Feedback Controller design (Ackermann's Formula) engane cheyyam? പോൾ പ്ലേസ്‌മെന്റ് സ്റ്റേറ്റ് ഫീഡ്‌ബാക്ക് കൺട്രോളർ ഡിസൈൻ (അക്കർമാൻസ് ഫോർമുല) വിശദീകരിക്കുക

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$K = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 0 & \dots & 1 \end{bmatrix} Q_c^{-1} \alpha_c(A), \quad u(t) = -K\mathbf{x}(t) + r(t)$$

Pole placement shifts open-loop poles to desired locations; requires controllability matrix $Q_c$ to have full rank $n$.

📌 ⚡ Ackermann's Formula
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$K = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 0 & \dots & 1 \end{bmatrix} Q_c^{-1} \phi_d(A), \quad u = -K\mathbf{x} + r$$

Ormavekkan: Closed-loop poles desired locations-ilottu move cheyyan state feedback $u = -K\mathbf{x}$ use cheyyunnu. $\phi_d(A)$ desired characteristic polynomial-il matrix $A$ substitute cheythathaanu.

⚡ ⚡ അക്കർമാൻസ് സമവാക്യം
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$K = [0 \quad \dots \quad 1] Q_c^{-1} \phi_d(A)$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: പോളുകളെ ഇഷ്ടമുള്ള സ്ഥാനങ്ങളിലേക്ക് മാറ്റി സിസ്റ്റത്തെ സ്റ്റേബിൾ ആക്കാനുള്ള ഫീഡ്‌ബാക്ക് ഗെയിൻ മാട്രിക്സ് ($K$).

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Control Objective:
Design a state feedback gain vector $K = \begin{bmatrix} k_1 & k_2 & \dots & k_n \end{bmatrix}$ such that the control law $u(t) = -K\mathbf{x}(t) + r(t)$ places the closed-loop system poles at arbitrarily chosen locations $\mu_1, \mu_2, \dots, \mu_n$.

2. Prerequisite Condition:
The system must be completely state controllable: $$\operatorname{rank}(Q_c) = \operatorname{rank}\begin{bmatrix} B & AB & A^2 B & \dots & A^{n-1}B \end{bmatrix} = n$$

3. Step-by-Step Design Procedure using Ackermann's Formula:

  1. Specify Desired Closed-Loop Poles: Choose desired locations $\mu_1, \mu_2, \dots, \mu_n$ based on transient specifications (settling time $t_s$, peak overshoot $M_p$).
  2. Formulate Desired Characteristic Polynomial: $$\alpha_c(s) = (s - \mu_1)(s - \mu_2)\dots(s - \mu_n) = s^n + \alpha_{n-1}s^{n-1} + \dots + \alpha_1 s + \alpha_0$$
  3. Evaluate Matrix Polynomial $\alpha_c(A)$: Substitute matrix $A$ into the desired polynomial: $$\alpha_c(A) = A^n + \alpha_{n-1}A^{n-1} + \dots + \alpha_1 A + \alpha_0 I$$
  4. Calculate Controllability Matrix & Inverse: $$Q_c = \begin{bmatrix} B & AB & A^2 B & \dots & A^{n-1}B \end{bmatrix}_{n \times n} \implies \text{Compute } Q_c^{-1}$$
  5. Compute Feedback Gain Vector $K$ using Ackermann's Formula: $$K = \begin{bmatrix} 0 & 0 & \dots & 0 & 1 \end{bmatrix}_{1 \times n} Q_c^{-1} \alpha_c(A)$$
  6. Closed-Loop Verification: The closed-loop state matrix is $A_{\text{cl}} = (A - BK)$. Verify that $\det(sI - (A - BK)) = \alpha_c(s)$.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

Design Steps:

  1. Check controllability: $\det(Q_c) \ne 0$.
  2. Write desired characteristic polynomial from given poles $\mu_1, \dots, \mu_n$: $\phi_d(s) = (s - \mu_1)\dots(s - \mu_n) = s^n + \alpha_{n-1}s^{n-1} + \dots + \alpha_0$.
  3. Form matrix $\phi_d(A) = A^n + \alpha_{n-1}A^{n-1} + \dots + \alpha_0 I$.
  4. Calculate $K = [0 \;\; 0 \dots 1] Q_c^{-1} \phi_d(A)$.

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

Think of balancing an inverted broom on your palm. Without moving your hand, the broom falls over (unstable pole in right-half plane). But if you watch the broom's angle and angular speed ($\mathbf{x}$) and react by moving your hand ($u = -K\mathbf{x}$), you can keep it standing upright! Ackermann's formula gives you the exact sensitivity knob $K$ so the broom responds with the speed and smoothness you want.

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

Oru unstable boat-il aalukal irikkunna karyam alochikku: Boat chavitti aadumbol, aalukal thirichu weight shift cheythu ($u = -Kx$) boat-ine stable aakki nirthunnu. Athupole unstable poles-ine namukku ishtamulla stable sthalathekku maati nirthunna technique aanu pole placement!

🇮🇳 മലയാളം ▼

ആടിയുലയുന്ന ബോട്ടിനെ ആളുകൾ ബാലൻസ് ചെയ്തു നിർത്തുന്നത് പോലെ അൺസ്റ്റേബിൾ സിസ്റ്റത്തെ സ്റ്റേബിളാക്കുന്ന വിദ്യ!

7/14 Marks (Long Essay / Derivation)
Long Derivation ID: ACS-Q15

Explain the design procedure of a full-order Luenberger state observer and derive its error dynamics. Full-Order State Observer (Luenberger Observer) engane design cheyyam? ഫുൾ-ഓർഡർ സ്റ്റേറ്റ് ഒബ്സർവർ (Luenberger Observer) ഡിസൈൻ വിശദീകരിക്കുക

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$\dot{\hat{\mathbf{x}}} = A\hat{\mathbf{x}} + B\mathbf{u} + L(\mathbf{y} - C\hat{\mathbf{x}}), \quad \dot{\mathbf{e}} = (A - LC)\mathbf{e}$$

Observer reconstructs unmeasurable internal states; error $\mathbf{e}(t) \to \mathbf{0}$ if $(A - LC)$ is designed to be asymptotically stable.

⚡ ⚡ Observer Dynamics & Error Equation
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\dot{\hat{\mathbf{x}}} = A\hat{\mathbf{x}} + B\mathbf{u} + L(\mathbf{y} - C\hat{\mathbf{x}}), \quad \dot{\mathbf{e}} = (A - LC)\mathbf{e}$$

Ormavekkan: Output measurement error $(\mathbf{y} - C\hat{\mathbf{x}})$ observer-lekku feedback koduthu states estimate cheyyunnu. Observer poles controller poles-inekkalum 2 to 5 times faster aayirikkannam!

⚡ ⚡ ഒബ്സർവർ എറർ സമവാക്യം
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\dot{\mathbf{e}} = (A - LC)\mathbf{e}$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: അളക്കാൻ കഴിയാത്ത ആന്തരിക അവസ്ഥകളെ ഔട്ട്പുട്ട് ഫീഡ്‌ബാക്ക് വഴി കണക്കാക്കാൻ ഒബ്സർവർ സഹായിക്കുന്നു.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Purpose of a State Observer:

  • State feedback control requires knowing all state variables $\mathbf{x}(t)$. In practice, many states (like internal flux or winding temperature) cannot be measured directly due to cost or physical constraints.
  • A full-order observer is a software algorithm running in parallel with the plant that estimates all $n$ state variables $\hat{\mathbf{x}}(t)$ using only measured inputs $\mathbf{u}(t)$ and outputs $\mathbf{y}(t)$.

2. Observer Mathematical Structure:
$$\dot{\hat{\mathbf{x}}}(t) = A\hat{\mathbf{x}}(t) + B\mathbf{u}(t) + L\left( \mathbf{y}(t) - \hat{\mathbf{y}}(t) \right)$$ $$\hat{\mathbf{y}}(t) = C\hat{\mathbf{x}}(t)$$ where $\hat{\mathbf{x}}(t)$ is the estimated state vector, and $L$ is the $n \times 1$ observer gain matrix.

3. Derivation of Observer Error Dynamics:

  • Define state estimation error as: $\mathbf{e}(t) = \mathbf{x}(t) - \hat{\mathbf{x}}(t)$.
  • Differentiate with respect to time: $$\dot{\mathbf{e}}(t) = \dot{\mathbf{x}}(t) - \dot{\hat{\mathbf{x}}}(t)$$
  • Substitute plant equations and observer equations: $$\dot{\mathbf{e}}(t) = [A\mathbf{x} + B\mathbf{u}] - [A\hat{\mathbf{x}} + B\mathbf{u} + L(\mathbf{y} - C\hat{\mathbf{x}})]$$ $$\dot{\mathbf{e}}(t) = A(\mathbf{x} - \hat{\mathbf{x}}) - L(C\mathbf{x} - C\hat{\mathbf{x}}) = A\mathbf{e} - LC\mathbf{e}$$ $$\dot{\mathbf{e}}(t) = (A - LC)\mathbf{e}(t)$$

4. Design Condition & Ackermann's Observer Formula:

  • For the estimation error to decay rapidly to zero ($\mathbf{e}(t) \to \mathbf{0}$ as $t \to \infty$), the observer matrix $(A - LC)$ must be asymptotically stable.
  • The system must be completely state observable ($\operatorname{rank}(Q_0) = n$).
  • Using duality, the observer gain $L$ is calculated using Ackermann's formula: $$L = \alpha_0(A) Q_0^{-1} \begin{bmatrix} 0 \\ 0 \\ \vdots \\ 1 \end{bmatrix}_{n \times 1}$$ Rule of thumb: Observer poles are designed to be 3 to 5 times faster than controller poles so that state estimates converge before control action takes effect.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

1. Actual system: $\dot{\mathbf{x}} = A\mathbf{x} + B\mathbf{u}, \quad \mathbf{y} = C\mathbf{x}$.

2. Observer estimate: $\dot{\hat{\mathbf{x}}} = A\hat{\mathbf{x}} + B\mathbf{u} + L(\mathbf{y} - \hat{\mathbf{y}})$, where $\hat{\mathbf{y}} = C\hat{\mathbf{x}}$.

3. Estimation error $\mathbf{e}(t) = \mathbf{x}(t) - \hat{\mathbf{x}}(t) \implies \dot{\mathbf{e}} = \dot{\mathbf{x}} - \dot{\hat{\mathbf{x}}} = (A - LC)\mathbf{e}$.

4. Design $L$ such that eigenvalues of $(A - LC)$ are in the far left-half $s$-plane so error decays rapidly to zero!

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

An observer is like a co-pilot running a flight simulator inside the computer. The simulator copies the plane's flight physics. Every millisecond, the simulator checks its simulated altitude against the real altitude sensor. If there is an error, it uses gain $L$ to nudge the simulated numbers until the simulation matches reality perfectly!

Module 4

Non-Linear Systems & Describing Function Analysis

5 Elaborated University Questions & Answers

3 Marks (Short Answer)
Short Answer ID: ACS-Q16

Explain the common physical non-linear phenomena observed in non-linear control systems that do not occur in linear systems. Non-Linear control systems-nte main characteristics & physical phenomena enthokke aanu? നോൺ-ലീനിയർ സിസ്റ്റങ്ങളുടെ പ്രത്യേകതകളും സവിശേഷ പ്രതിഭാസങ്ങളും എന്തൊക്കെയാണ്?

Watch Video Solution
📌 Remember This
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage

Superposition fails; exhibits limit cycles, jump resonance, subharmonic oscillations, and frequency-dependent stability.

📌 ⚡ 5 Unique Non-Linear Phenomena
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$\text{Superposition Fails: } f(x_1 + x_2) \ne f(x_1) + f(x_2), \quad f(\alpha x) \ne \alpha f(x)$$

Ormavekkan: Linear systems-il kaanatha 5 phenomena: Jump Resonance, Subharmonic Oscillations, Limit Cycles (sustained oscillations without external input), Frequency-dependent stability, and Multiple Equilibrium points.

📌 ⚡ നോൺ-ലീനിയർ പ്രത്യേകതകൾ
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$\text{Superposition തത്വം ബാധകമല്ല}$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: ലിമിറ്റ് സൈക്കിളുകൾ, ജമ്പ് റെസൊണൻസ്, സബ്-ഹാർമോണിക് ഓസിലേഷനുകൾ എന്നിവ നോൺ-ലീനിയർ സിസ്റ്റങ്ങളിൽ മാത്രം കാണപ്പെടുന്നു.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

Non-linear systems do not satisfy the principle of superposition and exhibit unique physical phenomena that never occur in linear systems:

  1. Failure of Superposition Principle: Homogeneity and additivity fail ($f(x_1 + x_2) \ne f(x_1) + f(x_2)$). System response depends strongly on the amplitude of input excitation, not just its frequency.
  2. Limit Cycles: Stable or unstable self-sustained periodic oscillations of fixed amplitude and frequency that occur without any external periodic input (e.g., relay chattering in thermostats).
  3. Jump Resonance: Discontinuous jump in output amplitude and phase when excitation frequency is smoothly swept across a critical resonant band (commonly seen in saturating iron-core inductors).
  4. Subharmonic Oscillations: The system produces output frequencies that are integer fractions of the input frequency ($\frac{\omega}{2}, \frac{\omega}{3}$).
  5. Multiple Equilibrium States: While linear systems have a single isolated equilibrium point at the origin, non-linear systems can have multiple stable and unstable equilibrium points.

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide
  • 1. Superposition fails: Response to sum of inputs is not the sum of individual responses.
  • 2. Limit Cycles: Fixed amplitude and frequency-il self-sustained oscillations (neither grows to infinity nor dies to zero).
  • 3. Jump Resonance: Input frequency smooth aayi vary cheyyumbol output amplitude-il sudden discontinuous jump undaakunnu.
  • 4. Subharmonic Oscillations: Input frequency $\omega$ aaneel output-il $\omega/2, \omega/3$ frequencies kaanunnu.
  • 5. Multiple Isolated Singular Points: Linear system-il single equilibrium point matram; non-linear-il multiple stable/unstable points varum.
📝 Exact English Text for University Examination:

Non-linear systems do not satisfy the principle of superposition and exhibit unique physical phenomena that never occur in linear systems:

  1. Failure of Superposition Principle: Homogeneity and additivity fail ($f(x_1 + x_2) \ne f(x_1) + f(x_2)$). System response depends strongly on the amplitude of input excitation, not just its frequency.
  2. Limit Cycles: Stable or unstable self-sustained periodic oscillations of fixed amplitude and frequency that occur without any external periodic input (e.g., relay chattering in thermostats).
  3. Jump Resonance: Discontinuous jump in output amplitude and phase when excitation frequency is smoothly swept across a critical resonant band (commonly seen in saturating iron-core inductors).
  4. Subharmonic Oscillations: The system produces output frequencies that are integer fractions of the input frequency ($\frac{\omega}{2}, \frac{\omega}{3}$).
  5. Multiple Equilibrium States: While linear systems have a single isolated equilibrium point at the origin, non-linear systems can have multiple stable and unstable equilibrium points.

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പ്രാദേശിക പഠന സഹായി

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പഠന സഹായി

സൂപ്പർപൊസിഷൻ പരാജയപ്പെടുന്നതും, ലിമിറ്റ് സൈക്കിളുകൾ, ജമ്പ് റെസൊണൻസ് തുടങ്ങിയ പ്രതിഭാസങ്ങളും വിശദീകരിക്കുക.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

Non-linear systems do not satisfy the principle of superposition and exhibit unique physical phenomena that never occur in linear systems:

  1. Failure of Superposition Principle: Homogeneity and additivity fail ($f(x_1 + x_2) \ne f(x_1) + f(x_2)$). System response depends strongly on the amplitude of input excitation, not just its frequency.
  2. Limit Cycles: Stable or unstable self-sustained periodic oscillations of fixed amplitude and frequency that occur without any external periodic input (e.g., relay chattering in thermostats).
  3. Jump Resonance: Discontinuous jump in output amplitude and phase when excitation frequency is smoothly swept across a critical resonant band (commonly seen in saturating iron-core inductors).
  4. Subharmonic Oscillations: The system produces output frequencies that are integer fractions of the input frequency ($\frac{\omega}{2}, \frac{\omega}{3}$).
  5. Multiple Equilibrium States: While linear systems have a single isolated equilibrium point at the origin, non-linear systems can have multiple stable and unstable equilibrium points.

Figure: Figure 4.1: Distinctive Non-Linear Dynamic Phenomena (Jump Resonance & Hysteresis)

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

If you push a swing gently, it moves a little; if you push twice as hard, a linear system says it goes twice as high. But in reality, if you push too hard, the chains go slack or the swing flips over the top bar! That's non-linearity: doubling the input does not double the result, and strange things like sudden jumps or permanent rattling (limit cycles) appear.

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

Linear system oru obedient student pole aanu — 1 hour padichaal 10 mark, 2 hours padichaal 20 mark. Pakshe Non-linear system oru mood ulla aalaanu — kurachu neram nannayi kekkum, sudden aayi jump cheyyum, thante thanne frequency-il aadi kondirikkum (Limit cycle)!

💡 ലളിതമായി മനസ്സിലാക്കാം — പ്രാദേശിക ഉദാഹരണം
🇮🇳 മലയാളം ▼

ഇൻപുട്ടിന് ആനുപാതികമല്ലാതെ പെട്ടെന്ന് വലിയ മാറ്റങ്ങൾ കാണിക്കുന്ന സ്വഭാവമാണിത്!

3 Marks (Short Answer)
Short Answer ID: ACS-Q16B

Derive the describing function for an ideal relay and a relay with dead-zone non-linearity. Ideal Relay-udeyum Relay with Dead-Zone-nteyum Describing Function derive cheyyuka. ഐഡിയൽ റിലേയുടെയും ഡെഡ്-സോൺ റിലേയുടെയും ഡിസ്ക്രൈബിംഗ് ഫങ്ഷൻ കണ്ടുപിടിക്കുക

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$N(X)_{\text{ideal}} = \frac{4M}{\pi X}, \quad N(X)_{\text{deadzone}} = \frac{4M}{\pi X}\sqrt{1 - \left(\frac{D}{X}\right)^2}$$

Describing function $N(X) = Y_1 / X$; represents equivalent fundamental harmonic gain of non-linearity.

📌 ⚡ Relay Describing Functions
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$N(X)_{\text{Ideal}} = \frac{4M}{\pi X}, \quad N(X)_{\text{Dead-Zone}} = \frac{4M}{\pi X}\sqrt{1 - \left(\frac{D}{X}\right)^2} \quad (X \ge D)$$

Ormavekkan: Non-linearity-lekku sinusoidal input $x(t) = X \sin(\omega t)$ koduthu fundamental harmonic output $Y_1$ Fourier series vechu kandupidikkunnathaanu Describing Function $N(X) = \frac{Y_1}{X}$.

📌 ⚡ റിലേ ഡിസ്ക്രൈബിംഗ് ഫങ്ഷൻ
⏱️ 5 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$N(X)_{\text{Ideal}} = \frac{4M}{\pi X}$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: ഫൂറിയർ സീരീസ് ഉപയോഗിച്ച് ഔട്ട്പുട്ടിന്റെ ഫണ്ടമെന്റൽ ഹാർമോണിക് ഘടകം കണ്ടെത്തുന്നു.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Concept of Describing Function:
The describing function $N(X)$ approximates a non-linear element by the ratio of the fundamental harmonic component of its output $y_1(t)$ to a sinusoidal input $x(t) = X \sin\omega t$: $$N(X) = \frac{Y_1 \angle \phi_1}{X} = \frac{A_1 + j B_1}{X}$$

2. Ideal Relay (On-Off Element):

  • Output jumps between $+M$ and $-M$. The output waveform is a symmetrical square wave of amplitude $M$.
  • Fourier analysis yields fundamental peak $Y_1 = \frac{4M}{\pi}$ with zero phase shift ($\phi_1 = 0$): $$N(X) = \frac{4M}{\pi X}$$
  • Gain drops inversely with input amplitude $X$.

3. Relay with Dead-Zone:

  • For input $|x(t)| \le D$, output is zero. For $|x(t)| > D$, output is $+M$ or $-M$.
  • Switching angle: $\theta = \sin^{-1}\left(\frac{D}{X}\right)$ (requires $X > D$ for any output).
  • Describing Function: $$N(X) = \frac{4M}{\pi X} \cos\theta = \frac{4M}{\pi X} \sqrt{1 - \left(\frac{D}{X}\right)^2} \quad \text{for } X \ge D$$ $$N(X) = 0 \quad \text{for } X < D$$

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

1. Ideal Relay: Output square wave with height $\pm M$. Fourier fundamental coefficient $B_1 = \frac{4}{\pi}\int_0^{\pi/2} M \sin\theta d\theta = \frac{4M}{\pi}$. Hence $N(X) = \frac{B_1}{X} = \frac{4M}{\pi X}$. Phase angle $0^\circ$.

2. Relay with Dead-Zone $D$: Relay switches only when $X \sin\theta \ge D \implies \theta_1 = \sin^{-1}(D/X)$. Fundamental $B_1 = \frac{4M}{\pi}\cos\theta_1 = \frac{4M}{\pi}\sqrt{1 - (D/X)^2}$. Hence $N(X) = \frac{4M}{\pi X}\sqrt{1 - (D/X)^2}$.

📝 Exact English Text for University Examination:

1. Concept of Describing Function:
The describing function $N(X)$ approximates a non-linear element by the ratio of the fundamental harmonic component of its output $y_1(t)$ to a sinusoidal input $x(t) = X \sin\omega t$: $$N(X) = \frac{Y_1 \angle \phi_1}{X} = \frac{A_1 + j B_1}{X}$$

2. Ideal Relay (On-Off Element):

  • Output jumps between $+M$ and $-M$. The output waveform is a symmetrical square wave of amplitude $M$.
  • Fourier analysis yields fundamental peak $Y_1 = \frac{4M}{\pi}$ with zero phase shift ($\phi_1 = 0$): $$N(X) = \frac{4M}{\pi X}$$
  • Gain drops inversely with input amplitude $X$.

3. Relay with Dead-Zone:

  • For input $|x(t)| \le D$, output is zero. For $|x(t)| > D$, output is $+M$ or $-M$.
  • Switching angle: $\theta = \sin^{-1}\left(\frac{D}{X}\right)$ (requires $X > D$ for any output).
  • Describing Function: $$N(X) = \frac{4M}{\pi X} \cos\theta = \frac{4M}{\pi X} \sqrt{1 - \left(\frac{D}{X}\right)^2} \quad \text{for } X \ge D$$ $$N(X) = 0 \quad \text{for } X < D$$

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പ്രാദേശിക പഠന സഹായി

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പഠന സഹായി

സ്ക്വയർ വേവ് ഔട്ട്പുട്ടിന്റെ ഫണ്ടമെന്റൽ കോഫിഷ്യന്റ് $B_1 = \frac{4M}{\pi}$ കണ്ടുപിടിച്ച് $N(X) = \frac{B_1}{X}$ എഴുതുക.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

1. Concept of Describing Function:
The describing function $N(X)$ approximates a non-linear element by the ratio of the fundamental harmonic component of its output $y_1(t)$ to a sinusoidal input $x(t) = X \sin\omega t$: $$N(X) = \frac{Y_1 \angle \phi_1}{X} = \frac{A_1 + j B_1}{X}$$

2. Ideal Relay (On-Off Element):

  • Output jumps between $+M$ and $-M$. The output waveform is a symmetrical square wave of amplitude $M$.
  • Fourier analysis yields fundamental peak $Y_1 = \frac{4M}{\pi}$ with zero phase shift ($\phi_1 = 0$): $$N(X) = \frac{4M}{\pi X}$$
  • Gain drops inversely with input amplitude $X$.

3. Relay with Dead-Zone:

  • For input $|x(t)| \le D$, output is zero. For $|x(t)| > D$, output is $+M$ or $-M$.
  • Switching angle: $\theta = \sin^{-1}\left(\frac{D}{X}\right)$ (requires $X > D$ for any output).
  • Describing Function: $$N(X) = \frac{4M}{\pi X} \cos\theta = \frac{4M}{\pi X} \sqrt{1 - \left(\frac{D}{X}\right)^2} \quad \text{for } X \ge D$$ $$N(X) = 0 \quad \text{for } X < D$$

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

An ideal relay is like a light switch: you flip it slightly, and BAM, it clicks fully ON. A relay with a dead-zone is like a loose light switch: you have to push it past a loose wiggle zone ($D$) before it finally clicks ON. The describing function calculates the "effective gain" of this switch when excited by a sine wave.

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

Oru geyser thermostat nokku: Choodu kurayumbol ON aavunnu, choodu koodumbol OFF aavunnu. Ithu square wave tharum. Square wave-le fundamental sine wave matram filter cheythu edukkunna mathematical glass aanu Describing Function!

സ്ക്വയർ വേവ് ഔട്ട്പുട്ടിൽ നിന്നും പ്രധാനപ്പെട്ട സൈൻ വേവ് ഘടകത്തെ മാത്രം അരിച്ചെടുക്കുന്ന രീതിയാണിത്!

7/14 Marks (Long Essay / Derivation)
Long Derivation ID: ACS-Q17

Derive the describing function expression N(X) for a symmetrical saturation non-linearity. Symmetrical Saturation non-linearity-ude Describing Function derive cheyyuka. സിമെട്രിക്കൽ സാച്ചുറേഷൻ നോൺ-ലീനിയാരിറ്റിയുടെ ഡിസ്ക്രൈബിംഗ് ഫങ്ഷൻ സമവാക്യം കണ്ടെത്തുക

Watch Video Solution
⚡ Key Equation
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time ⭐ High Mark Weightage
$$N(X) = \frac{2k}{\pi} \left[ \sin^{-1}\left(\frac{S}{X}\right) + \frac{S}{X}\sqrt{1 - \left(\frac{S}{X}\right)^2} \right] \quad (X > S)$$

For $X \le S$, element is linear with $N(X) = k$; for $X > S$, equivalent gain decreases monotonically as $1/X$.

📌 ⚡ Saturation Describing Function
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🌴 Manglish Tone
$$N(X) = \frac{2k}{\pi}\left[\sin^{-1}\left(\frac{S}{X}\right) + \frac{S}{X}\sqrt{1 - \left(\frac{S}{X}\right)^2}\right] \quad (X > S)$$

Ormavekkan: Input amplitude $X \le S$ aaneel no saturation, $N(X) = k$ (pure linear gain). $X > S$ aavumbol amplitude koodumthorum gain $N(X)$ kuranju kuranju varum!

⚡ ⚡ സാച്ചുറേഷൻ സമവാക്യം
⏱️ 12 Mins Writing Time 🇮🇳 മലയാളം
$$N(X) = \frac{2k}{\pi}\left[\sin^{-1}\left(\frac{S}{X}\right) + \frac{S}{X}\sqrt{1 - \left(\frac{S}{X}\right)^2}\right]$$

ഓർത്തു വെക്കാൻ: ഇൻപുട്ട് പരിധി വിടുമ്പോൾ ഔട്ട്പുട്ട് ഫ്ലാറ്റായി കട്ട് ചെയ്യപ്പെടുന്നു. ആംപ്ലിറ്റ്യൂഡ് കൂടുന്തോറും ഗെയിൻ കുറയുന്നു.

Section A Exam-Ready Answer

1. Non-Linear Characteristic:
Consider a symmetrical saturation non-linearity with linear slope $k$ and saturation limit $S$:

$$y(t) = \begin{cases} k x(t), & -S \le x(t) \le S \\ k S, & x(t) > S \\ -k S, & x(t) < -S \end{cases}$$

2. Applied Sinusoidal Excitation:
Let input be $x(t) = X \sin\theta$, where $\theta = \omega t$.
If $X \le S$, the system operates purely in the linear region, so $N(X) = k$.
If $X > S$, the output saturates when $X \sin\theta_1 = S \implies \theta_1 = \sin^{-1}\left(\frac{S}{X}\right)$.

3. Fourier Fundamental Component Derivation:
Due to odd quarter-wave symmetry ($y(\theta) = -y(-\theta)$ and $y(\pi - \theta) = y(\theta)$): $$A_1 = 0 \quad \text{(zero phase shift)}$$ $$B_1 = \frac{4}{\pi} \int_0^{\pi/2} y(\theta) \sin\theta \, d\theta$$ Split the integration interval into the linear zone ($0 \to \theta_1$) and the saturation zone ($\theta_1 \to \pi/2$): $$B_1 = \frac{4}{\pi} \left[ \int_0^{\theta_1} (k X \sin\theta) \sin\theta \, d\theta + \int_{\theta_1}^{\pi/2} (k S) \sin\theta \, d\theta \right]$$

Step 1: Evaluate First Integral (Linear Zone):
$$\int_0^{\theta_1} \sin^2\theta \, d\theta = \left[ \frac{\theta}{2} - \frac{\sin 2\theta}{4} \right]_0^{\theta_1} = \frac{\theta_1}{2} - \frac{\sin\theta_1 \cos\theta_1}{2}$$

Step 2: Evaluate Second Integral (Saturation Zone):
$$\int_{\theta_1}^{\pi/2} \sin\theta \, d\theta = [-\cos\theta]_{\theta_1}^{\pi/2} = \cos\theta_1$$

Step 3: Combine and Substitute $S = X \sin\theta_1$:
$$B_1 = \frac{4k X}{\pi} \left[ \frac{\theta_1}{2} - \frac{\sin\theta_1 \cos\theta_1}{2} \right] + \frac{4k (X \sin\theta_1)}{\pi} \cos\theta_1$$ $$B_1 = \frac{2k X}{\pi} \theta_1 - \frac{2k X}{\pi} \sin\theta_1 \cos\theta_1 + \frac{4k X}{\pi} \sin\theta_1 \cos\theta_1$$ $$B_1 = \frac{2k X}{\pi} \left[ \theta_1 + \sin\theta_1 \cos\theta_1 \right]$$

4. Final Describing Function:
Since $\sin\theta_1 = \frac{S}{X}$ and $\cos\theta_1 = \sqrt{1 - (S/X)^2}$: $$N(X) = \frac{B_1}{X} = \frac{2k}{\pi} \left[ \sin^{-1}\left(\frac{S}{X}\right) + \frac{S}{X}\sqrt{1 - \left(\frac{S}{X}\right)^2} \right] \quad \text{for } X > S$$ $$N(X) = k \quad \text{for } X \le S$$

🌴 Manglish Local Tone Study Guide

🌴 Manglish Study Guide

1. Saturation threshold angle $\theta_1 = \sin^{-1}(S/X)$.

2. Output waveform is sinusoidal up to $\theta_1$, then clipped flat at $kS$ from $\theta_1$ to $\pi/2$.

3. Fundamental Fourier integral: $B_1 = \frac{4}{\pi}\left[\int_0^{\theta_1} k X \sin^2\theta d\theta + \int_{\theta_1}^{\pi/2} k S \sin\theta d\theta\right]$.

4. Evaluating integrals gives: $N(X) = \frac{B_1}{X} = \frac{2k}{\pi}[\theta_1 + \sin\theta_1 \cos\theta_1] = \frac{2k}{\pi}[\sin^{-1}(S/X) + \frac{S}{X}\sqrt{1 - (S/X)^2}]$.

📝 Exact English Text for University Examination:

1. Non-Linear Characteristic:
Consider a symmetrical saturation non-linearity with linear slope $k$ and saturation limit $S$:

$$y(t) = \begin{cases} k x(t), & -S \le x(t) \le S \\ k S, & x(t) > S \\ -k S, & x(t) < -S \end{cases}$$

2. Applied Sinusoidal Excitation:
Let input be $x(t) = X \sin\theta$, where $\theta = \omega t$.
If $X \le S$, the system operates purely in the linear region, so $N(X) = k$.
If $X > S$, the output saturates when $X \sin\theta_1 = S \implies \theta_1 = \sin^{-1}\left(\frac{S}{X}\right)$.

3. Fourier Fundamental Component Derivation:
Due to odd quarter-wave symmetry ($y(\theta) = -y(-\theta)$ and $y(\pi - \theta) = y(\theta)$): $$A_1 = 0 \quad \text{(zero phase shift)}$$ $$B_1 = \frac{4}{\pi} \int_0^{\pi/2} y(\theta) \sin\theta \, d\theta$$ Split the integration interval into the linear zone ($0 \to \theta_1$) and the saturation zone ($\theta_1 \to \pi/2$): $$B_1 = \frac{4}{\pi} \left[ \int_0^{\theta_1} (k X \sin\theta) \sin\theta \, d\theta + \int_{\theta_1}^{\pi/2} (k S) \sin\theta \, d\theta \right]$$

Step 1: Evaluate First Integral (Linear Zone):
$$\int_0^{\theta_1} \sin^2\theta \, d\theta = \left[ \frac{\theta}{2} - \frac{\sin 2\theta}{4} \right]_0^{\theta_1} = \frac{\theta_1}{2} - \frac{\sin\theta_1 \cos\theta_1}{2}$$

Step 2: Evaluate Second Integral (Saturation Zone):
$$\int_{\theta_1}^{\pi/2} \sin\theta \, d\theta = [-\cos\theta]_{\theta_1}^{\pi/2} = \cos\theta_1$$

Step 3: Combine and Substitute $S = X \sin\theta_1$:
$$B_1 = \frac{4k X}{\pi} \left[ \frac{\theta_1}{2} - \frac{\sin\theta_1 \cos\theta_1}{2} \right] + \frac{4k (X \sin\theta_1)}{\pi} \cos\theta_1$$ $$B_1 = \frac{2k X}{\pi} \theta_1 - \frac{2k X}{\pi} \sin\theta_1 \cos\theta_1 + \frac{4k X}{\pi} \sin\theta_1 \cos\theta_1$$ $$B_1 = \frac{2k X}{\pi} \left[ \theta_1 + \sin\theta_1 \cos\theta_1 \right]$$

4. Final Describing Function:
Since $\sin\theta_1 = \frac{S}{X}$ and $\cos\theta_1 = \sqrt{1 - (S/X)^2}$: $$N(X) = \frac{B_1}{X} = \frac{2k}{\pi} \left[ \sin^{-1}\left(\frac{S}{X}\right) + \frac{S}{X}\sqrt{1 - \left(\frac{S}{X}\right)^2} \right] \quad \text{for } X > S$$ $$N(X) = k \quad \text{for } X \le S$$

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പ്രാദേശിക പഠന സഹായി

🇮🇳 മലയാളം പഠന സഹായി

ആംഗിൾ $\theta_1$ വരെ ലീനിയറായും ബാക്കി ഭാഗം കോൺസ്റ്റന്റായും ഇന്റഗ്രേറ്റ് ചെയ്ത് സമവാക്യം രൂപീകരിക്കുക.

📝 പരീക്ഷയിൽ എഴുതേണ്ട ഇംഗ്ലീഷ് വാചകങ്ങൾ:

1. Non-Linear Characteristic:
Consider a symmetrical saturation non-linearity with linear slope $k$ and saturation limit $S$:

$$y(t) = \begin{cases} k x(t), & -S \le x(t) \le S \\ k S, & x(t) > S \\ -k S, & x(t) < -S \end{cases}$$

2. Applied Sinusoidal Excitation:
Let input be $x(t) = X \sin\theta$, where $\theta = \omega t$.
If $X \le S$, the system operates purely in the linear region, so $N(X) = k$.
If $X > S$, the output saturates when $X \sin\theta_1 = S \implies \theta_1 = \sin^{-1}\left(\frac{S}{X}\right)$.

3. Fourier Fundamental Component Derivation:
Due to odd quarter-wave symmetry ($y(\theta) = -y(-\theta)$ and $y(\pi - \theta) = y(\theta)$): $$A_1 = 0 \quad \text{(zero phase shift)}$$ $$B_1 = \frac{4}{\pi} \int_0^{\pi/2} y(\theta) \sin\theta \, d\theta$$ Split the integration interval into the linear zone ($0 \to \theta_1$) and the saturation zone ($\theta_1 \to \pi/2$): $$B_1 = \frac{4}{\pi} \left[ \int_0^{\theta_1} (k X \sin\theta) \sin\theta \, d\theta + \int_{\theta_1}^{\pi/2} (k S) \sin\theta \, d\theta \right]$$

Step 1: Evaluate First Integral (Linear Zone):
$$\int_0^{\theta_1} \sin^2\theta \, d\theta = \left[ \frac{\theta}{2} - \frac{\sin 2\theta}{4} \right]_0^{\theta_1} = \frac{\theta_1}{2} - \frac{\sin\theta_1 \cos\theta_1}{2}$$

Step 2: Evaluate Second Integral (Saturation Zone):
$$\int_{\theta_1}^{\pi/2} \sin\theta \, d\theta = [-\cos\theta]_{\theta_1}^{\pi/2} = \cos\theta_1$$

Step 3: Combine and Substitute $S = X \sin\theta_1$:
$$B_1 = \frac{4k X}{\pi} \left[ \frac{\theta_1}{2} - \frac{\sin\theta_1 \cos\theta_1}{2} \right] + \frac{4k (X \sin\theta_1)}{\pi} \cos\theta_1$$ $$B_1 = \frac{2k X}{\pi} \theta_1 - \frac{2k X}{\pi} \sin\theta_1 \cos\theta_1 + \frac{4k X}{\pi} \sin\theta_1 \cos\theta_1$$ $$B_1 = \frac{2k X}{\pi} \left[ \theta_1 + \sin\theta_1 \cos\theta_1 \right]$$

4. Final Describing Function:
Since $\sin\theta_1 = \frac{S}{X}$ and $\cos\theta_1 = \sqrt{1 - (S/X)^2}$: $$N(X) = \frac{B_1}{X} = \frac{2k}{\pi} \left[ \sin^{-1}\left(\frac{S}{X}\right) + \frac{S}{X}\sqrt{1 - \left(\frac{S}{X}\right)^2} \right] \quad \text{for } X > S$$ $$N(X) = k \quad \text{for } X \le S$$

💡 Understand It Simply — Intuitive Explanation & Analogy
Learning Aid ▼

Think of an audio amplifier. If you speak softly, your voice comes out louder without distortion ($N(X) = k$). But if you shout into the microphone, the speaker clips the sound peaks flat (saturation). The describing function mathematically calculates how much the "effective amplification gain" drops as you push the speaker further into distortion.

💡 Understand It Simply (Manglish) — Local Campus Analogy
🌴 Local Tone ▼

Oru amplifier-il volume kooduthal vachaal paattu pottitherichu clip aavunnathu pole aanu Saturation. Input cheriya value aayirikkumbol full paattum neat aayi varum ($N=k$). Pakshe input valuthavumbol peaks thalachethi flat aakkunnu, so effective gain kurayunnu!
